in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
);
the product
(A_{1}^{v}-A_{0}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,1})\dots(A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{p_{v}-1}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,p_{v}})
of these configurations has the form
(A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{0}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
,
for some
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{0}^{v}})
(respectively,
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{0}^{v}},\breve{\partial})
): we then have, roughly speaking, that

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1}(\tau_{A_{0}^{v}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}))
recovers the part of
\mathfrak{c}_{v}
in the vertical
strip
(A_{0}^{v},A_{p_{v}}^{v})\times\mathbb{R}
.

We can define a map
g\colon D^{q}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
g\colon D^{q}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) by the formula

v\mapsto\Big(W_{i_{0}}(v),\dots,W_{i_{p_{v}}}(v);(A_{1}^{v}-A_{0}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,1}),\dots,(A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{p_{v}-1}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,p_{v}})\Big).

To see that
g
is continuous, note that if a weight
W_{i_{j}}(v)
goes to 0,
then the number
A^{v}_{j}
is dropped from the list
A_{0}^{v},\dots,A_{p_{v}}^{v}
and the following happens:

•

if
1\leq j\leq p_{v}-1
, then
the configurations
(A_{j}^{v}-A_{j-1}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,j})
and
(A_{j+1}^{v}-A_{j}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,j+1})
are replaced in the formula above by their product in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
), according to the identifications defining
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
); this is compatible with the fact that the configurations
\mathfrak{c}_{v,j}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{v,j+1}

are “adjacent” in
\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}
(in
\diamond
), and if
A^{v}_{j}
is dropped these two configurations are replaced in the construction by their “concatenation”
in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}_{A^{v}_{j-1},A^{v}_{j+1}})

(respectively,
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond{A^{v}_{j-1},A^{v}_{j+1}},\partial)
), which is up to canonical identifications the configuration
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{A^{v}_{j-1},A^{v}_{j+1}}}(\mathfrak{c}_{v})
;

•

if
j=0
or
j=p_{v}
, then the configuration
(A_{1}^{v}-A_{0}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,1})
or
(A_{p_{v}}^{v}-A_{p_{v}-1}^{v},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,p_{v}})

is dropped in the formula above, according to the identifications defining
of
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
); this is compatible with the fact that
the configuration
\mathfrak{c}_{v,0}
or
\mathfrak{c}_{v,p_{v}}
is also dropped in the construction, as soon as
A^{v}_{0}
or
A^{v}_{p_{v}}
is dropped.

Note also that
g
sends
\partial D^{q}
inside
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
(inside
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
): in fact,
if
f(v)=\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{lr}}
, then all configurations
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,j}
are supported on the empty set.
For
v\in D^{q}
we remark, moreover, the equalities
B^{v,-}-A_{0}^{v}=b^{-}(g(v))
and
B^{v,+}-A_{0}^{v}=b^{+}(g(v))
, by virtue of which the lower and upper barycentres of
g(v)
can be recovered from the numbers
B^{v,-}
and
B^{v,+}
and vice versa, once the “translation parameter”
A_{0}^{v}
is known; in particular,

B^{v,+}-B^{v,-}=b^{+}(g(v))-b^{-}(g(v))
.

We are left to prove that
\sigma g
is homotopic to
f
, relative to
\partial D^{q}
, i.e. they represent the same element in
\pi_{q}(\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}})

(in
\pi_{q}(\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}})
).

Definition 4.20
.

Recall Definitions
2.22
and
2.25
.
For all
0<t<1
we define homotopies
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}_{t},\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}_{t}\colon\mathbb{C}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}
by the following formula.

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{t}(z,s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s)+\big(\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s))\big)\sqrt{-1}&\mbox{if }z\notin\mathring{\diamond},\ \Im(z)\geq\frac{1}{2}\\
\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s)-\big(\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s))\big)\sqrt{-1}&\mbox{if }z\notin\mathring{\diamond},\ \Im(z)\leq\frac{1}{2}\\
\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s)-\left(\Im(z)-\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{t}(z,s))-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}\sqrt{-1}&\mbox{if }z\in\diamond,\ z\neq z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}},z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}.\end{array}\right.

Here
\bullet
is either
\mathrm{l}
or
\mathrm{r}
.

Roughly speaking,
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}_{t}
collapses the part of
\diamond
contained in
[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}

to
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
, and expands the other part
\diamond\setminus[0,t]\times\mathbb{R}
inside
\diamond
;
similar remarks hold for
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}_{t}
.

Note that for
z\in\diamond
close to
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
we have the equalities
\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{l}}_{t}(z,s))-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}=s

and
\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{r}}_{t}(z,s))-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}=\frac{-s+st}{1-s+st}
.
Similarly, for
z\in\diamond
close to
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
we have
\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{l}}_{t}(z,s))-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}=\frac{st}{1-st}

and
\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{r}}_{t}(z,s))-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}{\frac{1}{2}-\mathfrak{d}^{\diamond}(z)}=s
.
In particular, the homotopies
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}_{t},\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}_{t}
are continuous; note also that they depend continuously on
t\in(0,1)
, so that
we can define continuous maps

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}\,,\,\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}\,\colon\mathbb{C}\times[0,1]\times(0,1)\to\mathbb{C}

by
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}(z,s,t)=\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}_{t}(z,s)

and
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}(z,s,t)=\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}_{t}(z,s)
.
Note also that, for
\bullet=\mathrm{l},\mathrm{r}
, the following properties hold:

(1)

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{t}(-,s)
is an endomorphism of the nice couple
(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})

(respectively,
(\diamond,\partial)
) for all
(s,t)\in[0,1]\times(0,1)
;

(2)

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{t}(-,s)

fixes pointwise the subspaces

\mathbb{C}_{\Re\leq 0}
and
\mathbb{C}_{\Re\geq 1}
, and preserves the subspaces
\diamond
,

[0,1]\times\left(-\infty,\frac{1}{2}\right]\setminus\mathring{\diamond}

and
[0,1]\times\left[\frac{1}{2},\infty\right)\setminus\mathring{\diamond}
;
in particular it fixes the points
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}},z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
;

(3)

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{t}(-,0)=\Id{C}
for all
t\in(0,1)
.

It follows from property (1) and
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 4.4]
that for
\bullet=\mathrm{l},\mathrm{r}
there
is an induced map

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})\times[0,1]\times(0,1)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})

\left(\mbox{respectively,}\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)\times[0,1]\times(0,1)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)\ \right).

Property (2) ensures that for all
(s,t)\in[0,1]\times(0,1)
the map
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}(-,s,t)

restricts to a self-map of the subspace
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}

(respectively,
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
), and is equivariant with respect to the
G\times G^{\mathrm{op}}
action
on this subspace. In particular there is an induced map

\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}\times[0,1]\times(0,1)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}

\left(\mbox{respectively, }\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}\times[0,1]\times(0,1)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}\ \right).

Property (3) ensures that
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\bullet}_{*}(-,0,t)
is the identity
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
(of
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
)
for all
t\in(0,1)
. We can now define a map

H\colon D^{q}\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
(respectively,
H\colon D^{q}\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
)
by setting

H(v,s)=\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{l}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\diamond,\mathrm{r}}_{*}(f(v),s,B^{v,+}),s,\frac{B^{v,-}}{B^{v,+}}\right),

where we recall that
0<B^{v,-}<B^{v,+}<1
.
By the construction of
g
we have
H(-,1)=\sigma g
. This concludes the proof that

\sigma_{*}
is surjective on
\pi_{q}
.

In the particular case
q=0
we obtain that, since

B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
and
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
are connected, then
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
and
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}

are also connected.
The fact that
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
is connected can also be proved by combining
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 6.16 and Proposition 7.10]
, Theorem
2.19
and Lemma
2.24
: the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
is the quotient of the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
by the action of
G\times G^{\mathrm{op}}
, and on
\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}})\cong G
this action can be identified with the action by left and right multiplication, which is transitive. The fact that
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
is connected is instead new in our discussion, although it could have been proved directly using simpler arguments.

4.3.
Injectivity on homotopy groups

For
q\geq 0
we want now to prove that
\sigma_{*}\colon\pi_{q}(B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})\to\pi_{q}(\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}})
(respectively,

\sigma_{*}\colon\pi_{q}(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}})\to\pi_{q}(\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}})
) is injective.
We fix a basepoint
*\in S^{q}\subset D^{q+1}
and start with a pointed map

\tilde{f}\colon S^{q}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
\tilde{f}\colon S^{q}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) and a map
f\colon D^{q+1}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}

(respectively,
f\colon D^{q+1}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
), such that the restriction of
f
on
S^{q}=\partial D^{q+1}
is equal to
\sigma\tilde{f}
.

We can construct a map
g\colon D^{q+1}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
g\colon D^{q+1}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) in the same way
as we constructed
g\colon D^{q}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
g\colon D^{q}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) in the previous subsection:
using compactness of
D^{q+1}
, we can find a suitable cover
V_{1},\dots,V_{r}
of
D^{q+1}
and
disjoint intervals
J_{1},\dots,J_{r}\subset(0,1)
, ordered from left to right, such that
for all
v\in V_{i}
, if
\mathfrak{c}_{v}
is a representative of
f(v)=[\mathfrak{c}_{v}]_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
, then
\mathfrak{c}_{v}
is supported on a set
P_{v}
with
\Re(P_{v})\cap J_{i}=\emptyset
.
We also fix
A_{i}\in J_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
, and a partition of unity
W_{1},\dots W_{r}

subordinate to the covering
V_{i}
; again we assume that for all
v\in D^{q+1}
there are at least
two indices
1\leq i\leq r
such that
W_{i}(v)>0
.
The rest of the construction is the same as in the previous subsection; note that,
in general,
g(*)
is a point in the contractible subspace

B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
(respectively,
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
), but
g(*)
is not necessarily the basepoint
of
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(of
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).
For our scopes it suffices to prove that
g|_{S^{q}}
is homotopic to
\tilde{f}

as maps
S^{q}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(as maps
S^{q}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
), through a homotopy sending
*\in S^{q}

inside
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
(respectively,
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
) at all times. We are thus replacing
\pi_{q}(B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})
(respectively,
\pi_{q}(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}})
) with the set of homotopy classes of maps of pairs from
(S^{q},*)
to
(B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}},B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset})
(respectively, to
(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}},B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
).

At this point of the discussion it becomes convenient to switch our focus to the thin bar construction. Recall Definition
3.8
:
the projection
p_{\bar{B}}\colon B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}\to\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
p_{\bar{B}}\colon B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) is a weak homotopy equivalence; similarly, note that the subspace
\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}\subset\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,

\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}\subset\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) is contractible.
Therefore, it suffices to
prove that
p_{\bar{B}}\circ g|_{S^{q}}
and
p_{\bar{B}}\circ\tilde{f}
are homotopic as maps

S^{q}\to\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(as maps
S^{q}\to\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
), by a homotopy sending
*\in S^{q}
inside

\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
(inside
\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
) at all times.
Exhibiting such homotopy will be much easier than comparing
g|_{S^{q}}
and
\tilde{f}
directly.

One of the advantages of the thin bar construction occurs
already in the construction of the map
g\colon D^{q+1}\to B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}

(respectively,
g\colon D^{q+1}\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).
Recall that we had to shrink the
intervals
J_{1},\dots,J_{r}
to make them disjoint, in order to ensure that
the numbers
A_{1},\dots,A_{r}
are all distinct.
This was crucial when defining
g(v)
for
v\in D^{q+1}
(or, in the previous
subsection, for
v\in D^{q}
): we started from the list of indices
1<i_{0}<\dots<i_{p_{v}}<l
satisfying
W_{i_{j}}(v)>0
; we
denoted by
A_{0}^{v},\dots,\dots A_{p_{v}}^{v}
the list
A_{i_{0}},\dots,A_{i_{p_{v}}}
, and used the portions of
f(v)
contained in
the slices
\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}_{A_{j-1}^{v},A_{j}^{v}}
(in
(\diamond_{A_{j-1}^{v},A_{j}^{v}},\partial)
) to define the configurations
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,1},\dots,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,{p_{v}}}
. The fact that the numbers
A_{1},\dots,A_{r}

are all distinct was crucial in ensuring that all slices have strictly positive width and,
most important, the numbers
W_{i_{0}}(v),\dots,W_{i_{p_{v}}}(v)
naturally form an
ordered

list of
p+1
numbers: this is crucial, as we want to use these numbers to define the barycentric coordinates of a point in
\Delta^{p}
.

Suppose instead that we repeat the above construction of
g
, but using directly the thin bar construction; in other words, consider the composition of
g
with the projection
p_{\bar{B}}
: then the fact that
A_{1},\dots,A_{r}
are all distinct
ceases to be important. Indeed, suppose that for some
v\in D^{q+1}

we write a list
A_{0}^{v},\dots,\dots A_{p_{v}}^{v}
as above, and suppose that for some

1\leq j\leq p_{v}
we have
A^{v}_{j-1}=A^{v}_{j}
:
this means, in particular, that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,j}=(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1})
.
On the one hand, we cannot unequivocally determine which of the barycentric coordinates

W_{i_{j-1}}(v)
and
W_{i_{j}}(v)
should come first in the list of barycentric
coordinates for
g(v)
; on the other hand, the two possibilities
give rise to the same configuration in
\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).
For simplicity, in the following we keep assuming that the numbers

0<A_{1}<\dots<A_{r}<1
are distinct, and we keep considering
g

and
\tilde{f}
as maps with values in
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).

Fix
v\in S^{q}
and let
\tilde{f}(v)\in B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
\tilde{f}(v)\in B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
)
be represented, for some
\tilde{p}_{v}\geq 0
,
by the
\tilde{p}_{v}
-tuple
(t^{v}_{1},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{1}),\dots,(t^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}})

of configurations in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
), with barycentric coordinates
w^{v}_{0},\dots,w^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}
.
Let the numbers
a^{v}_{0},\dots,a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}
and the barycentres
b_{v}
,
b_{\varepsilon,v}^{-}
and
b_{\varepsilon,v}^{+}
be computed
as in Definitions
4.4
and
4.5
with
respect to
(t^{v}_{1},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{1}),\dots,(t^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}})
and
w^{v}_{0},\dots,w^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}
.

Using the notation
from Subsection
4.2
, for
v\in S^{q}

let
1\leq i_{0}<\dots<i_{p_{v}}\leq r
be the list of all indices

i_{j}
satisfying
W_{i_{j}}(v)>0
; again let
A_{0}^{v},\dots,\dots A_{p_{v}}^{v}

denote the corresponding list of numbers
0<A_{i_{0}}<\dots<A_{i_{p_{v}}}<1
.
Recall that
g(v)\in B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
g(v)\in B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) is constructed using
the numbers
W_{i_{0}}(v),\dots,W_{i_{p^{\prime}}}(v)
as barycentric coordinates, and using
the portions of
\mathfrak{c}_{v}
contained in the slices
[A_{i_{0}},A_{i_{1}}]\times\mathbb{R},\dots,[A_{i_{p^{\prime}-1}},A_{i_{p^{\prime}}}]\times\mathbb{R}
to obtain configurations
(A_{i_{j}}-A_{i_{j-1}},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{v,j})
in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).

For
0\leq j\leq p_{v}
define
\alpha^{v}_{j}=b^{-}_{\varepsilon,v}+(b^{+}_{\varepsilon,v}-b^{-}_{\varepsilon,v})A^{v}_{j}
.
Then
b^{-}_{\varepsilon,v}\leq\alpha^{v}_{0}\leq\dots\leq\alpha^{v}_{p^{\prime}}\leq b^{+}_{\varepsilon,v}
, and
the inequality
b^{-}_{\varepsilon,v}<b^{+}_{\varepsilon,v}
is strict.
To fix notation, let

\beta^{v}_{0}\leq\dots\leq\beta^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}

be the union of the lists of numbers

a^{v}_{0},\dots,a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}
and
\alpha^{v}_{0},\dots,\alpha^{v}_{p_{v}}
: all numbers
\beta^{v}_{j}
,
as well as the numbers
b_{v}
,
b^{-}_{\varepsilon,v}
and
b^{+}_{\varepsilon,v}
, belong to the interval
[a^{v}_{0}-\varepsilon,a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}+\varepsilon]=[-\varepsilon,a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}+\varepsilon]
.

In writing the list
\beta^{v}_{0}\leq\dots\leq\beta^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}
we
choose
a shuffle of the sets

\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}\right\}
and
\left\{0,\dots,p_{v}\right\}
into
\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1\right\}
, i.e. a pair of strictly increasing and commonly surjective maps

\tilde{\eta}\colon\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}\right\}\to\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1\right\},\quad\eta\colon\left\{0,\dots,p_{v}\right\}\to\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1\right\}.

In the generic case the numbers
a^{v}_{0},\dots,a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}},\alpha^{v}_{0},\dots,\alpha^{v}_{p_{v}}
are all distinct
and the choice of the shuffle is unique,
but nothing prevents that, for some
v\in S^{q}
, some of these numbers become equal.

Let
\hat{w}^{v}_{0},\dots,\hat{w}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}

denote the corresponding shuffle of the lists of barycentric coordinates,
i.e.
\hat{w}^{v}_{\tilde{\eta}(j)}=w^{v}_{j}
and
\hat{w}^{v}_{\eta(j)}=W_{i_{j}}(v)
. Define
also

\mathbf{e}\colon\left\{0,\dots,\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1\right\}\times[0,1]\to[0,1],\quad\quad\mathbf{e}\colon(\tilde{\eta}(j),s)\mapsto s,\quad\mathbf{e}\colon(\eta(j),s)\mapsto 1-s.

Let
(a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}},\mathfrak{c})
be the product
(t^{v}_{1},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{1})\dots(t^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}},\mathfrak{c}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}})
in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
), and use Notation

2.2
. Then for all
0\leq j\leq\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1
the vertical line
\mathbb{C}_{\Re=\beta_{j}}
is disjoint from
P
.
We can then cut the rectangle
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}}
(respectively,
\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{a^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}}
) along these vertical lines, and define configurations

\bar{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{j}
in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{\beta_{j-1},\beta_{j}})
(in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\beta_{j-1},\beta_{j}},\breve{\partial})
)
as the parts of
\mathfrak{c}
lying in the regions
\mathbb{S}_{\beta_{j-1},\beta_{j}}
, for all
1\leq j\leq\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1
: formally we evaluate the restriction maps
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{\beta_{j-1},\beta_{j}}}
on
\mathfrak{c}
.
Let
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{j}
be the configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{\beta_{j}-\beta_{j-1}})
(in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\beta_{j}-\beta_{j-1}},\breve{\partial})
) given by
(\tau_{-\beta_{j-1}})_{*}(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{j})
, for all
1\leq j\leq\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1
.

We define a homotopy
H\colon S^{q}\times[0,1]\to\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
H\colon S^{q}\times[0,1]\to\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
)
by the formula

\begin{split}H(v,s)=&\Big(\ \mathbf{e}(0,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{0}\,,\,\dots\,,\,\mathbf{e}(\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}\,;\\
&\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{1}\right)\,,\,\dots\,,\,\left(\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}-\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}\right)\Big).\end{split}

The continuity of the formula relies on the fact that we are using the thin bar construction: if
varying
v\in S^{q}
two consecutive values
\beta_{j-1}
and
\beta_{j}
become equal, then the corresponding
configuration
(\beta_{j}-\beta_{j-1},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{j-1})
becomes equal to
(0,(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1}))
and
can, thus, be dropped from the list: the weights
\mathbf{e}(j-1,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{j-1}
and

\mathbf{e}(j,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{j}
are replaced by their sum
\mathbf{e}(j-1,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{j-1}+\mathbf{e}(j,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{j}

and we obtain a description of the same configuration in
\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) which
is formally
symmetric
in the indices
j-1
and
j
.

For
s=1
the list of weights
\mathbf{e}(0,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{0},\dots,\mathbf{e}(p+p^{\prime}+1,s)\hat{w}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}

reduces to the list of weights
w^{v}_{0},\dots,w^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}}
, shuffled with
p_{v}+1
occurrences of
0
; if we drop the zeros and perform the corresponding products of consecutive elements in the list

\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{1}\right),\dots,\left(\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}-\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}\right)
, we recover
\tilde{f}(v)
.

Similarly, for
s=0
we obtain the weights
W_{i_{1}}(v),\dots,W_{i_{p_{v}}}(v)
shuffled with
\tilde{p}_{v}+1
occurrences of
0
; in particular, since
\beta_{0}=a_{0}=0
and
\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}=a_{p}
, at least one zero at the beginning and at least one zero at the end of the list of all weights are dropped. If we perform
the corresponding products of consecutive elements in the list

\left(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{1}\right),\dots,\left(\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}-\beta_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}},\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{\tilde{p}_{v}+p_{v}+1}\right)
, and if we drop the corresponding elements at the two ends of the list,
we recover
g(v)
.

Finally, note that for
v=*\in S^{q}
we have that
all configurations
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{v}_{j}
are supported on the empty set, so that
H(v,-)
is a path in
\bar{B}\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
(in
\bar{B}\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{\emptyset}
). This concludes the proof that
\sigma_{*}
is injective
on homotopy groups.

4.4.
Homology of the group completion of
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}

The second part of Theorem
4.1
implies, together with Theorem
3.4
, that there is a weak equivalence

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}\simeq\Omega\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}.

If we select one connected component on each side, using Theorem
2.19

for the left-hand side, we obtain a weak equivalence

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}.

By
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 6.16]
we have that
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
is a (disconnected) covering of

\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
: more precisely, there is a free and properly discontinuous action of
G\times G^{\mathrm{op}}
on the former space, and the latter is the quotient by this action. Hence also the connected component
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}\subseteq\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
is a covering of
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
, with deck transformation group given by the stabiliser in
G\times G^{\mathrm{op}}
of this component, which is the “diagonal” copy of
G
, consisting of pairs
(g,g^{-1,op})
for varying
g\in G
. We obtain a weak equivalence

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}},

and Lemmas
2.7
and
2.24
and
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 7.10]
yield a weak equivalence

\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\diamond,\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}.

Now we use the first part of Theorem
4.1
, together with the fact that
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
is a covering
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
, again by
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 6.16]
; taking one component of loop spaces we obtain a weak equivalence

\Omega_{0}B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}.

Putting the above weak equivalences together, we obtain

\Omega_{0}B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}\simeq\Omega_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}\simeq\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\diamond,\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}.

Finally, Theorem
3.7
(which is applicable thanks to Lemma
3.6
) and Theorem
2.15
,
imply the following homology isomorphism,

H_{*}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})[\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})^{-1}]\cong\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})]\otimes H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\diamond,\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}).

Here
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
denotes the enveloping group of the PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
, as in
[
Bia21
, Definition 2.9]
: concretely, this is the group generated by elements
[a]
for
a
ranging in
\mathcal{Q}
, under the relations
[a][b]=[b][a^{b}]
for all
a,b\in\mathcal{Q}
, and
[a]\hat{b}=\widehat{ab}
for all
a,b\in\mathcal{Q}
such that the product
ab
is already defined in
\mathcal{Q}
. In fact
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
is the universal group receiving a map of PMQs from
\mathcal{Q}
; moreover,
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
coincides with the enveloping group of both the monoid
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
and its free unitalisation
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}\sqcup\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}\cong\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})
.

In fact, the combination of Theorems
2.19
,
3.4
and
4.1
implies the following isomorphisms of discrete monoids (which happen to be groups):

\pi_{1}(\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}})\cong\pi_{1}(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}})\cong\pi_{0}(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+})\cong G.

On the other hand
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 6.16]
implies that
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
is a covering of
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
with group of deck transformations
G
, and
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
is connected by Theorem
2.19
. In the next lemma we prove that
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
is, in fact, the universal cover of
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
.

Lemma 4.21
.

The space
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
is simply connected.

Proof.

Recall the weak equivalence
\sigma\colon B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\mathbbl{1}}
. For
g\in G
let
(1,\mathfrak{c}_{g})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
be the pair with
\mathfrak{c}_{g}
being a configuration supported on the unique point
\frac{1}{2}
, carrying local monodromy
g\in G
. Consider the loop
\gamma_{g}\colon[0,1]\to B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
sending
t\in[0,1]
to the class of
(t,1-t;(1,\mathfrak{c}_{g}))\in\Delta^{1}\times\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\subset\coprod_{p\geq 0}\Delta^{p}\times\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{p}
in the quotient. Observe that the class of
\gamma_{g}
in
\pi_{1}(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}})
corresponds to the class of
(1,\mathfrak{c}_{g})\in\pi_{0}(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+})
, which along the total monodromy corresponds to the element
g\in G
; in particular every element of
\pi_{1}(B\breve{\mathrm{HM}})
is represented by a loop
\gamma_{g}
.

Recall now Definition
2.22
and the proof of Theorem
2.19
, and let
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}_{g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}_{G}}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
be the configuration supported on the set
\left\{0,\frac{1}{2},1\right\}
, such that the
G
-valued total monodromy sends small loops spinning clockwise around
0,\frac{1}{2},1
to
g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}
, respectively.
Let
\hat{\gamma}_{g}\colon[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
be the path defined by

\hat{\gamma}_{g}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{l}}_{1/2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{c}}_{g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}_{G}},1\right)&\mbox{if }0\leq t\leq 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\\[5.69046pt]
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{l}}_{1/2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{c}}_{g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}_{G}},1-\frac{1-2(1-t)^{2}}{2t(1-t)}\right)&\mbox{if }1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\leq t\leq\frac{1}{2};\\[5.69046pt]
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{r}}_{1/2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{c}}_{g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}_{G}},\frac{1-2(1-t)^{2}}{2t(1-t)}-1\right)&\mbox{if }\frac{1}{2}\leq t\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}};\\[5.69046pt]
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{r}}_{1/2}\left(\hat{\mathfrak{c}}_{g^{-1},g,\mathbbl{1}_{G}},1\right)&\mbox{if }\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\leq t\leq 1.\end{array}\right.

Consider also the map
(\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1})_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}
induced by
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1}
;
then
\tilde{\gamma}_{g}:=(\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1})_{*}\circ\hat{\gamma}_{g}\colon[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}
is a path lifting
the loop
\sigma\circ\gamma_{g}\colon[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}
along the covering
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
.

Both configurations
\tilde{\gamma}_{g}(0)
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{g}(1)
are supported on
\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}
; the local monodromies around
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
are
\mathbbl{1}_{G}
and
\mathbbl{1}_{G}
, respectively, for the first configuration, and are
g^{-1}
and
g
, respectively, for the second. It follows that the path
\tilde{\gamma}_{g}
is a loop if and only if
g=\mathbbl{1}_{G}
. This shows that
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{\mathrm{op}}}
is a universal covering and, in particular,

\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}};\mathbbl{1}}
is simply connected.
∎

From now on it is convenient to replace the nice couple
(\diamond,\partial)
with the nice couple
(\mathcal{R},\partial)
.
For this, fix an orientation-preserving, semialgebraic homeomorphism
\xi\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}

fixing
*
and restricting to a homeomorphism of couples
(\diamond,\partial)\cong(\mathcal{R},\partial)
;
then
\xi
induces a homeomorphism
\xi_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)\cong\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)
, restricting
to a homeomorphism
\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\diamond,\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}\cong\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}
. Using again

[
Bia23a
, Proposition 7.10]
we can then replace
\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}

by the weakly equivalent space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
, where
0\in\partial\mathcal{R}
is the lower left
vertex.

We rephrase the last homology isomorphism, together with the discussion about simply connectedness,
as the following theorem.

Theorem 4.22
.

In the hypotheses of Theorem
4.1
there is an isomorphism of graded abelian groups

H_{*}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q}))[\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q}))^{-1}]\cong\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})]\otimes H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}).

Moreover, the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is simply connected.

We immediately observe that the left-hand side of the isomorphism in Theorem
4.22
only depends on the PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
, and not on the PMQ-group pair
(\mathcal{Q},G)
(in particular, not on the group
G
).
In fact the isomorphism of Theorem
4.22
is an isomorphism of rings, if we consider on

\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})]\otimes H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}})
the correct
structure of twisted tensor product of rings, which we briefly describe in the following.

The group
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
acts on the right on
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
by global conjugation: in fact,
G
acts on the right on this space by global conjugation,
and we consider the map of groups
\mathcal{G}(\mathfrak{e})\colon\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})\to G
. Consequently,
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
acts on the right
on
H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}})
by automorphisms of rings.

For
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}})

we define the twisted product
(g_{1}\otimes x_{1})\cdot(g_{2}\otimes x_{2}):=(g_{1}g_{2}\otimes(x_{1}^{g_{2}}\cdot x_{2})
.
This assignment extends to an associative product on
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})]\otimes H_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{0}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{0;\mathbbl{1}})
,
and with this ring structure the isomorphism of Theorem
4.22
is an isomorphism of rings.

The isomorphism of Theorem
4.22
is a bit surprising
at first glance, because
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q})

is
not
, in general, weakly equivalent to an
E_{2}
-algebra.

5.
The space
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)

For concrete homology computations the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is too large.
In this section we introduce a homotopy equivalent subspace

\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}};

under the assumption that
\mathcal{Q}
is
augmented
.
If, in addition, we assume that
\mathcal{Q}
is
normed
,
then
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
admits a natural filtration by closed subspaces. In the next
section, assuming further that
\mathcal{Q}
is finite and
rationally Poincaré
,
we will exploit this filtration to compute explicitly the rational cohomology
ring of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.

Recall from
[
Bia21
, Definitions 4.1 and 4.9]
that a PMQ is
augmented
if the set
\mathcal{Q}_{+}:=\mathcal{Q}\setminus\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}
is an ideal for the partial product, i.e. if for all
a,b\in\mathcal{Q}
such that
ab=\mathbbl{1}
we have
a,b=\mathbbl{1}
. A
normed
PMQ is a PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
together with a morphism of PMQs
N\colon\mathcal{Q}\to\mathbb{N}
such that
N^{-1}(0)=\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}
. Every normed PMQ is also augmented.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
and use Notation
2.2
, so that the support

P
of
\mathfrak{c}
splits as

\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{l}\right\}\subset\mathring{\mathcal{R}}

and
\left\{z_{l+1},\dots,z_{k}\right\}\subset\partial\mathcal{R}
.
Let
\beta\subset\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
be a clockwise oriented simple closed curve in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\setminus P

such that the disc bounded by
\beta
contains all points
z_{1},\dots,z_{l}
.

The configuration
\mathfrak{c}
lies in the subspace
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
if
the conjugacy class of
\mathfrak{G}(P)
corresponding to
\beta
is contained
in the PMQ
\mathfrak{Q}^{\mathrm{ext}}(P)_{\psi}\subset\mathfrak{G}(P)

(see
[
Bia23a
, Definition 2.13]
).

If
\mathcal{Q}
is augmented, we define
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
as the intersection

\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G):=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)\cap\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}.

Roughly speaking, a configuration
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
lies in
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)

if, using Notation
2.2
, the
l
values of
the monodromy
\psi
around the
l
points of
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
can be multiplied in
\mathcal{Q}
.
See Figure
7
.

*
\beta
\bullet
g_{6}
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
a_{1}
g_{4}
a_{2}
\bullet
\bullet
g_{5}
a_{3}

Figure 7.
The configuration
\mathfrak{c}
lies in the space
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
if
g_{6}\mathfrak{e}(a_{1})g_{4}\mathfrak{e}(a_{2})g_{5}\mathfrak{e}(a_{3})=\mathbbl{1}\in G
, none of
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}
is equal to
\mathbbl{1}\in\mathcal{Q}
, and the product
a_{1}a_{2}(a_{3}^{g_{5}^{-1}})
is defined in
\mathcal{Q}
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a nice couple and let
(\mathcal{Q},G)
be a PMQ-group pair with
\mathcal{Q}
augmented.
Let
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
; a point
z\in P
is
inert
for
\mathfrak{c}
if

z\in\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{Y}
and
\psi
sends to
\mathbbl{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}

each element of
\mathfrak{Q}(P)
represented by a small loop spinning clockwise around
z
.

If
\mathcal{Q}
is augmented, a configuration
\mathfrak{c}
lies in
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
if it lies in
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
and,
moreover, no point of the support of
\mathfrak{c}
is inert: in other words,
\psi
attains values different from
\mathbbl{1}
around all
l
points of
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
.

Example 5.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{Q}
is finite and normed, and let
N_{\mathrm{max}}\in\mathbb{N}
be the maximal
norm of an element of
\mathcal{Q}
. Let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
, and use Notation
2.2
.
Then at most
N_{\mathrm{max}}
of the
l
points in
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{1}
can be non-inert;
in particular if
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, then
l\leq N_{\mathrm{max}}
.
There is also another evident restriction on the behaviour of
\psi
:
if
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
is an admissible generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(P)
, then

\sum_{i=1}^{l}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq N_{\mathrm{max}}
.

Example 5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the abelian PMQ
\left\{\mathbbl{1},\bullet\right\}

with trivial partial multiplication, and let
G=\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}
be the trivial group.
Let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, and use Notation
2.2
.
Then
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
is either empty, or it contains exactly one point. We can define
a map
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{R}/\partial\mathcal{R}\cong S^{2}
by looking at the position of the unique
point of
P
in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
,
and taking the quotient point
[\partial\mathcal{R}]\in\mathcal{R}/\partial\mathcal{R}
if
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}=\emptyset
.
In fact, the map
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{R}/\partial\mathcal{R}
is a quasifibration with fibre
the Ran space
\mathrm{Ran}(\partial\mathcal{R})
, so it is a weak homotopy equivalence.

We will see in Proposition
5.5
that the inclusion
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is also a homotopy equivalence; hence, in this case, Theorem
4.22
reduces to a classical result of Segal
[
Seg73
]
stating that the group completion of the topological monoid

\coprod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Conf}_{n}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is
\Omega^{2}S^{2}
. Passing from

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
to its subspace
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
should thus be regarded as the analogue
of passing from the relative configuration space
\mathrm{Conf}(\mathcal{R},\partial)
to the sphere

\mathring{\mathcal{R}}/\partial\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
by a scanning argument.

5.1.
Deformation retraction onto
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)

In this subsection we will prove that
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
admits a deformation
retraction onto its subspace
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
; if
\mathcal{Q}
is augmented, the same argument will give by
restriction a deformation retraction
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
onto its subspace
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.
Using
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 7.4]
, we will therefore obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 5.5
.

For any PMQ-group pair
(\mathcal{Q},G)
the inclusion of

\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
into
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is a homotopy equivalence. If
\mathcal{Q}
is augmented,
the following is a square of inclusions which are homotopy equivalences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}

The rough idea of the proof of Proposition
5.5
is that each configuration
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
can be gradually magnified around the centre
z_{\mathrm{c}}\in\mathcal{R}
, letting gradually more and more points collide with
\partial\mathcal{R}
: such points are downgraded to points in the support of
\mathfrak{c}
around which only the
G
-valued monodromy is defined and they remain fixed during further magnification. At some finite time we obtain a configuration satisfying the properties of Definition
5.1
, and we stop the magnification.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
z_{0}\in\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
. For
z\in\mathbb{C}
let
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(z)\in[1,\infty]
be the infimum of all
s\geq 1

such that
z_{0}+s(z-z_{0})\notin\mathcal{R}
; note that
\mathfrak{d}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(z)=\infty
if and only if
z=z_{0}
.
We define a map
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}\colon\mathbb{C}\times[1,\infty)\to\mathbb{C}
by the formula

\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(z,s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}z&\mbox{if }z\notin\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\\
z_{0}+s(z-z_{0})&\mbox{if }z\in\mathcal{R}\mbox{ and }z_{0}+s(z-z_{0})\in\mathcal{R}\\
z_{0}+\mathfrak{d}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(z)\cdot(z-z_{0})&\mbox{if }z\in\mathcal{R}\mbox{ and }z_{0}+s(z-z_{0})\notin\mathcal{R}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Roughly speaking, the map
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(-,s)

expands the square
\frac{s-1}{s}z_{0}+\frac{1}{s}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
, which has side length
\frac{1}{s}
,
to the entire
\mathcal{R}
, by a homothety centred at
z_{0}
of rescaling factor
s
and collapses

\mathcal{R}\setminus(\frac{s-1}{s}z_{0}+\frac{1}{s}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
onto
\partial\mathcal{R}
.
Note that for all
s\geq 1
the map
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(-,s)
is an endomorphism of the nice couples
(\mathcal{R},\partial)
;
note also that
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(0,s)=0
for all
s\geq 1
, and that
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}}(-,1)
is the identity of
\mathbb{C}
. Thus we obtain a continuous map

(\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}})_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\times[1,\infty)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}},

such that
(\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{0}})_{*}(-,1)
is the identity of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
.

Definition 5.7
.

Let
z_{0}\in\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
. For each
0<\varepsilon<1
denote by
\beta_{z_{0},\varepsilon}\subset\mathring{\mathcal{R}}

the simple closed curve whose support is the square

(1-\varepsilon)z_{0}+\varepsilon(\partial\mathcal{R})
, i.e. the boundary of the square of side length
\varepsilon
obtained from
\partial\mathcal{R}
by a homothety centred at
z_{0}

of rescaling factor
\varepsilon
; we orient
\beta_{z_{0},\varepsilon}
clockwise.
Let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial)_{\mathbbl{1}}
and use Notation
2.2
.
We denote by
\mathfrak{W}_{z_{0}}(\mathfrak{c})\in[0,1]
the supremum of all
0<\varepsilon<1
satisfying
the following properties:

•

P\cap\beta_{z_{0},\varepsilon}=\emptyset
;

•

every element
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathfrak{G}(P)
in the conjugacy class corresponding to
\beta_{z_{0},\varepsilon}

belongs to
\mathfrak{Q}^{\mathrm{ext}}(P)_{\psi}\subseteq\mathfrak{G}(P)
.

Note that
\mathfrak{W}_{z_{0}}(\mathfrak{c})\leq 1
for all
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{\mathbbl{1}}
; moreover,

\mathfrak{W}_{z_{0}}(\mathfrak{c})=1
if and only if
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
.
Note, on the other hand, that
\mathfrak{W}_{z_{0}}(\mathfrak{c})>0
, as for a generic and very small
\varepsilon>0
the curve

\beta_{z_{0},\varepsilon}
is disjoint from
P
and encloses at most one point of
P
, so it corresponds to a conjugacy class of
\mathfrak{G}(P)

which is contained in
\mathfrak{Q}(P)\subset\mathfrak{Q}^{\mathrm{ext}}(P)_{\psi}
.
Note also that the assignment
\mathfrak{c}\mapsto\mathfrak{W}_{z_{0}}(\mathfrak{c})
is continuous in
\mathfrak{c}
.

Notation 5.8
.

Recall Notation
2.16
. We simplify the notation and write

\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}=\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{\mathrm{c}}}
and
\mathfrak{W}=\mathfrak{W}_{z_{\mathrm{c}}}
.

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

We define a homotopy

H^{\mathbb{B}}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}},

H^{\mathbb{B}}(\mathfrak{c},s)=\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c}\,,\,1-s+s\frac{1}{\mathfrak{W}(\mathfrak{c})}\right).

Note that
H^{\mathbb{B}}(-,0)
is the identity of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
,
that
H^{\mathbb{B}}(-,1)
takes values in
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
and that

H^{\mathbb{B}}(-,s)
restricts to the identity
of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)
for all
0\leq s\leq 1
.

This proves the first homotopy equivalence in the statement. The second homotopy
equivalence is completely analogous: we have constructed the deformation retraction
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
onto its subspace
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)
using enriched
functoriality with respect to
maps of nice couples; this implies that
H^{\mathbb{B}}(-,s)

restricts at all times to a self-map of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}

and, in particular, the restriction of
H^{\mathbb{B}}(-,1)
on
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
takes values
in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\cap\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q},G)=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.
∎

5.2.
Norm filtration

In the rest of the section we assume that
\mathcal{Q}
is endowed with a norm
N\colon\mathcal{Q}\to\mathbb{N}
. Our aim is to introduce
a filtration
F_{\bullet}
on
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
. It will be convenient to define
a norm filtration
F_{\bullet}
more generally on the Hurwitz space

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
associated with any nice couple
\mathfrak{C}
.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a nice couple, let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
and use Notation
2.2
.
Let
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
be an admissible generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(P)
.
We define a function of sets
N\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\to\mathbb{N}
by

N(\mathfrak{c})=N(\psi(f_{1}))+\dots+N(\psi(f_{l})),

and call
N(\mathfrak{c})
the
norm
of the configuration
\mathfrak{c}
.

For
\nu\geq 0
we define the
\nu
th
filtration layer
F_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)

as the subspace of configurations
\mathfrak{c}
with
N(\mathfrak{c})\leq\nu
. We also set

F_{-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)=\emptyset
.

For
\nu\geq 0
we denote by
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
the
\nu
th
filtration stratum

\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G):=F_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\setminus F_{\nu-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G).

Recall from
[
Bia23a
, Definition 2.5]
that given a nice couple
\mathfrak{C}=(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
and a finite subset
P\subset\mathcal{X}
, an
adapted covering
of
P
is a collection
\underline{U}
of disjoint, semialgebraic open discs in
\mathbb{C}
containing each a single point of
P
, and such that each point in
P\setminus\mathcal{Y}
is surrounded by a disc disjoint from
\mathcal{Y}
. The topology on
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
has a basis given by the open neighbourhoods
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})
, for varying

\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
and varying
\underline{U}
among adapted covers of
P
.

Lemma 5.10
.

For all
\nu\geq 0
we have that

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\setminus F_{\nu-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
is open in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
and assume
that
N(\mathfrak{c})\geq\nu
. Let
\underline{U}
be an adapted covering of
P
.
We claim that the open neighbourhood
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})

is contained in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\setminus F_{\nu-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})
, and use Notation
2.2
. For all
1\leq i\leq l

let
P^{\prime}_{i}=P^{\prime}\cap U_{i}
, and let
P^{\prime}_{i}=\left\{z^{\prime}_{i,1},\dots,z^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}}\right\}
.
Choose an admissible generating set
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
of
\mathfrak{G}(P)\cong\mathfrak{G}(\underline{U})
. We can regard
\mathfrak{G}(\underline{U})
as a subgroup of
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
. We can choose an admissible
generating set
f^{\prime}_{1},\dots,f^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}

of
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
with the following property: for all
1\leq i\leq l
,
if
f^{\prime}_{i,1},\dots,f^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}}
are the elements represented by loops spinning
around the points
z^{\prime}_{i,1},\dots,z^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}}
respectively, then the product

f^{\prime}_{i,1}\dots f^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}}
is equal to
f_{i}
in
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
.
The hypothesis
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})
implies the following equality in
\mathcal{Q}
, for all
1\leq i\leq l
:

\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i,1})\cdot\dots\cdot\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}})=\psi(f_{i}),

whence, using the norm on
\mathcal{Q}
, we obtain

N(\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i,1}))+\dots+N(\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i,k^{\prime}_{i}}))=N(\psi(f_{i})).

Summing over
1\leq i\leq l
, and recalling that
P^{\prime}_{1}\cup\dots\cup P^{\prime}_{l}
might
be a proper subset of
P^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{Y}=\left\{z^{\prime}_{1},\dots,z^{\prime}_{l^{\prime}}\right\}
, we obtain

\nu\leq N(\mathfrak{c})=\sum_{i=1}^{l}N(\psi(f_{i}))=\sum_{i=1}^{l}\sum_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}_{i}}N(\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i,j}))\leq\sum_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}N(\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i}))=N(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}),

which shows that
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})
is contained in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\setminus F_{\nu-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
.

∎

Notation 5.11
.

For every subspace
X\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
and for
\nu\geq-1
, we use the notation

F_{\nu}X=F_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\cap X
. For
\nu\geq 0
we use the notation

\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}X=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)\cap X
.

We will use Notation
5.11
mainly in the case

X=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)
.

5.3.
A model for
BG

Our next goal is to analyse the strata
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.
We start with
\mathfrak{F}_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, which can be identified with

\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R},\partial\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
. By
[
Bia23a
, Lemmas 5.4 and 5.5]

we can equivalently consider the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
,
where the group
G
is considered as a (complete) PMQ.
In this subsection we prove the following proposition.

Proposition 5.12
.

The space
\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is an Eilenberg-Maclane space of type
K(G,1)
.

Definition 5.13
.

We denote by
\sqcap\subset\partial\mathcal{R}
the union of the three closed sides of
\mathcal{R}

\sqcap:=\left\{0\right\}\times[0,1]\,\cup\,[0,1]\times\left\{1\right\}\,\cup\,\left\{1\right\}\times[0,1].

Lemma 5.14
.

The spaces
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
and
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
are contractible.

Proof.

Note that
\sqcap
is contractible;
more precisely we can fix a semialgebraic homotopy
\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}\colon\mathbb{C}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}

with the following properties:

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}(-,s)
is a
lax
endomorphism of the nice couple
(\sqcap,\emptyset)
, for all
0\leq s\leq 1
(see
[
Bia23a
, Definition 4.2]
);

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}(0,s)=0\in\mathbb{C}
for all
0\leq s\leq 1
;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}(-,0)=\Id_{\mathbb{C}}
;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}(-,1)
maps
\sqcap
constantly to
0
.

By functoriality, using that
G
is a complete PMQ, we obtain a homotopy

\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}.

Note that the map
\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}_{*}(-,1)
takes values in
\mathrm{Hur}(\left\{0\right\},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
,
which is just a point. Thus the homotopy
\mathcal{H}^{\sqcap}_{*}
exhibits

\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
as a contractible space.

By
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 7.10]
, the inclusion

\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}

is a homotopy equivalence, hence
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
is also contractible.
∎

Now let
\xi^{\sqcap}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
be a semialgebraic map with the following properties:

(1)

\xi^{\sqcap}
is a lax endomorphism of the nice couple
(\partial\mathcal{R},\emptyset)
, in particular it restricts to a map
\partial\mathcal{R}\to\partial\mathcal{R}
;

(2)

\xi^{\sqcap}
fixes
\mathbb{C}_{\Re\leq 0}
pointwise;

(3)

\xi^{\sqcap}
maps the horizontal segment
[0,1]\times\left\{0\right\}
constantly to
0
;

(4)

\xi^{\sqcap}
restricts to a homeomorphism
\mathbb{C}\setminus([0,1]\times\left\{0\right\})\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
.

Note that
\xi^{\sqcap}

is a lax morphism of nice couples
(\sqcap,\emptyset)\to(\partial\mathcal{R},\emptyset)
; we obtain by functoriality
a map
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
, see Figure
8
.

*
\bullet
g_{4}
\bullet
\bullet
g_{2}
g_{3}
\bullet
g_{1}
*
\bullet
\bullet
g^{\prime}_{4}
g^{\prime}_{5}
g^{\prime}_{4}g^{\prime}_{5}=g_{4}
\bullet
\bullet
g_{2}
g_{3}
\bullet
g_{1}

Figure 8.
Left: a configuration
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
. Right: a configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
in the fibre
(\xi^{\sqcap}_{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
.

We will prove that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
is a covering map.

Lemma 5.15
.

For
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
, the fibre of
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
over
\mathfrak{c}
is a non-empty and discrete subspace of
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
.

Proof.

Write
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi)
, where
P=\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{k}\right\}
and

\psi\colon\mathfrak{Q}_{(\partial\mathcal{R},\emptyset)}(P)\to G
is a map of PMQs;
without loss of generality suppose
z_{k}=0
.
Assume that we are given
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})\in(\xi^{\sqcap}_{*})^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})
.
Note that if
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})=\mathfrak{c}
, then, in particular,
\xi^{\sqcap}(P^{\prime})=P
and by properties (3) and (4) of
\xi^{\sqcap}
we must have
P^{\prime}=(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(P)\cap\sqcap\subset\mathbb{C}
.
To fix notation, let
z^{\prime}_{i}=(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(z_{i})\in P^{\prime}
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
, and let
z^{\prime}_{k}=0\in P^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}_{k+1}=1\in P^{\prime}
.

Fix an admissible generating set
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
for
\mathfrak{G}(P)
, and assume that
f_{k}

is represented by a loop supported in a small neighbourhood of the vertical segment
\left\{0\right\}\times[-1,0]\subset\mathbb{C}
joining
*
to 0. Then we can consider

(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}
as a map
\mathbb{C}\setminus P\to\mathbb{C}\setminus([0,1]\times\left\{0\right\}\cup P^{\prime})\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus P^{\prime}
, and map the generators
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}

to elements of
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
. Note that
f_{1},\dots,f_{k-1}
are mapped to simple loops spinning
clockwise around the points
z^{\prime}_{1},\dots,z^{\prime}_{k-1}
, whereas
f_{k}
is mapped to a simple loop
spinning clockwise around the segment
[0,1]\subset\mathbb{C}
. We decompose
(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}_{*}(f_{k})

as a product of two elements
f^{\prime}_{k},f^{\prime}_{k+1}
, represented by simple loops in
\mathbb{C}\setminus P^{\prime}
spinning
clockwise around
z^{\prime}_{k}
and
z^{\prime}_{k+1}

respectively, and define
f^{\prime}_{i}=(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}_{*}(f_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
: thus we obtain
an admissible generating set
f^{\prime}_{1},\dots,f^{\prime}_{k+1}
for
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
. Note that for
1\leq i\leq k-1
the generator
f^{\prime}_{i}
can be represented by a simple loop in
\mathbb{C}\setminus P^{\prime}
supported in
\mathbb{C}\setminus(P^{\prime}\cup[0,1]\times\left\{0\right\})
; similarly
f^{\prime}_{k}
and
f^{\prime}_{k+1}
can be represented by loops supported on small neighbourhoods of the straight segments in
\mathbb{C}
joining
*
with
0
and
1
respectively.

Since
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})=\mathfrak{c}
, we have in
G
the equalities
\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i})=\psi(f_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
, and

\psi(f_{k})=\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k+1})
.

Vice versa, for any factorisation of
\psi(f_{k})\in G
as the product
gh
of two elements in
G
,
we can define a configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
by setting
P^{\prime}=(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(P)\cap\sqcap
,
and by defining
\varphi^{\prime}
by sending
f^{\prime}_{i}\mapsto\psi(f_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq k-1
,
f^{\prime}_{k}\mapsto g
and
f^{\prime}_{k+1}\mapsto h
. This shows that

(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})
is non-empty.

Now note that, for
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
as above and for any adapted covering
\underline{U}^{\prime}
of
P^{\prime}
, we have that
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
is the unique configuration
in
\mathfrak{U}(P^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
supported on the set
P^{\prime}
. In fact, the normal neighbourhoods
\mathfrak{U}(\bar{\mathfrak{c}};\underline{U}^{\prime})
, for fixed
\underline{U}^{\prime}

and varying
\bar{\mathfrak{c}}
in
(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})
, are disjoint: compare with the proof of
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 3.8]
. This proves
that
(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(\mathfrak{c})
is a discrete topological space.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

There is a free action of
G
on
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
whose orbits are precisely the fibres of
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
, and write
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
with
P^{\prime}=\left\{z^{\prime}_{1},\dots,z^{\prime}_{k+1}\right\}
, where we assume
z^{\prime}_{k}=0
,
z^{\prime}_{k+1}=1
. For
g\in G
we can define a new configuration
g*\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},g*\psi^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}

by setting
g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i})=\psi^{\prime}(f_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
,

g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})=\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})g^{-1}
and
g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k+1})=g\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})
, where
f^{\prime}_{1},\dots,f^{\prime}_{k+1}
is an admissible generating set of
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})
as in the proof of Lemma
5.15
.

This defines a left action of
G
on the set
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
.
This action can also be obtained by identifying
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
with
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap,\sqcap;G,G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
via
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 5.4]
, and by considering
(0,(\sqcap,\sqcap),1)
as a left-right-based nice couple
[
Bia23a
, Definition 6.9]
, and by restricting the action of
G\times G^{op}
on
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap,\sqcap;G,G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
to the diagonal subgroup
G\subset G\times G^{op}
, which leaves the subspace
\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap,\sqcap;G,G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}
invariant. This proves, in particular, that the action is continuous.

For
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
as above, we use the notation
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi):=\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
, with
P=\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{k}\right\}
, and assume
z_{k}=0
and
z_{i}=\xi^{\sqcap}(z^{\prime}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
. Choose an admissible generating set
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
of
\mathfrak{G}(P)
as in Lemma
5.15
. Then
\psi(f_{i})=\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i})=g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
, and

\psi(f_{k})=\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})=\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})g^{-1}g\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})=\left(g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})\right)\left(g*\psi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{k})\right).

It follows that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})=\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(g*\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
.
∎

Lemma 5.17
.

The map
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
is open.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
be as in the proof of Lemma
5.15
, i.e.
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}\colon\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\mapsto\mathfrak{c}
, and let
\underline{U}^{\prime}
be an adapted covering of
P^{\prime}
. We want to find an adapted covering
\underline{U}
of
P

such that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{U}(P^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime}))
contains
\mathfrak{U}(P,\underline{U})
. We choose
\underline{U}
with the following
properties:

•

for all
1\leq i\leq k
the intersection
U_{i}\cap\partial
is contractible;

•

for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
the intersection
(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(U_{i})\cap\sqcap
is contained
in
U^{\prime}_{i}
;

•

the intersection
(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(U_{k})\cap\sqcap
is contained in
U^{\prime}_{k}\cup U^{\prime}_{k+1}
.

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}=(\tilde{P},\tilde{\psi})\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})
: we want to find a configuration
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\underline{U}^{\prime})

with
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Write
\tilde{P}=\left\{\tilde{z}_{1},\dots,\tilde{z}_{\tilde{k}}\right\}
, with
\tilde{z}_{\tilde{k}}=0
.
Then the finite set
\tilde{P}^{\prime}:=(\xi^{\sqcap})^{-1}(\tilde{P})\cap\sqcap
is contained in
\underline{U}^{\prime}
, and it intersects non-trivially
every component of
\underline{U}^{\prime}
. We write
\tilde{P}^{\prime}=\left\{\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}+1}\right\}
,
and assume
\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}}=0
and
\tilde{z}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}+1}=1
.

Let
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}+1}
be an admissible generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})

as in the proof of Lemma
5.15
. Note that we can regard
f^{\prime}_{k}
and
f^{\prime}_{k+1}

as elements of
\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})
by the composition
\mathfrak{G}(P^{\prime})\cong\mathfrak{G}(\underline{U}^{\prime})\subset\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})
;
moreover, the sequence of elements
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}-1},f^{\prime}_{k},f^{\prime}_{k+1}

is also a free generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})
, although in general it is not an admissible generating set: in fact,
f^{\prime}_{k}
can be decomposed as a product of distinct elements
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{i}
, with one element
equal to
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}}
, and similarly
f^{\prime}_{k+1}
can be decomposed as a product with one factor
equal to
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}+1}
.
Nevertheless we can define a morphism of groups
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P}^{\prime})\to G
by setting

\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\colon\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{i}\mapsto\tilde{\varphi}(\tilde{f}_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq\tilde{k}-1
,
and
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}\colon f^{\prime}_{i}\mapsto\varphi^{\prime}(f^{\prime}_{i})
for
i=k,k+1
. We can restrict
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}

to
\mathfrak{Q}_{(\sqcap,\emptyset)}(P^{\prime})
and obtain a morphism of PMQs
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{G}_{(\sqcap,\emptyset)}(P^{\prime})\to G
.

We can use the previous to define a configuration

\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}=(\tilde{P}^{\prime},\tilde{\psi}^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})
, satisfying
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
.
∎

In the last step of the proof, note that
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
is, in fact, the
unique
configuration
in
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U})
with
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
. This shows, in particular,
that for
\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
,
\underline{U}^{\prime}
and
\underline{U}
as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
,
there is a unique map of sets
\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})\to\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})
which is a section
of
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
, i.e. such that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}\circ\mathfrak{s}
is equal to the inclusion
of
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})
in
\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}
,
\mathfrak{c}
,
\underline{U}^{\prime}
and
\underline{U}
be as in the proof of Lemma
5.17
,
and let
\mathfrak{s}\colon\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})\to\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})
be the section defined above. Then
\mathfrak{s}
is continuous.

Proof.

Let
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})
, denote
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{s}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}})\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})
, and use the notation from the proof
of Lemma
5.17
. By continuity of
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
there is an adapted covering

\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime}
of
\tilde{P}^{\prime}
with
\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime}\subset\underline{U}^{\prime}
and such that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
maps

\mathfrak{U}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime};\tilde{\underline{U}})
inside
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})
.

First, note that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}

is injective on
\mathfrak{U}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime};\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime})
: for a configuration
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{U}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime};\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime})

we have, in fact,
\mathfrak{s}(\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime\prime}))=\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime\prime}
.

By Lemma
5.17
we know that
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
is open; it follows that
the map
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}\colon\mathfrak{U}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime};\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
is a homeomorphism
onto its image and, hence,
\mathfrak{s}
is continuous on the open set
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}(\mathfrak{U}(\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime};\tilde{\underline{U}}^{\prime}))
, which contains
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.12
.

The combination of Lemmas
5.15
,
5.17
and
5.18
shows that the map
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\sqcap;G)_{0,1;\mathbbl{1}}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}

is a covering. Lemma
5.14
shows that the total space is contractible, in particular connected, and Lemma
5.16
exhibits
G
as the group of deck transformations of
\xi^{\sqcap}_{*}
.
∎

Notation 5.19
.

We denote by
\mathcal{B}G
the space

\mathcal{B}G:=\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\cong\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R},\partial\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}=F_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)=\mathfrak{F}_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G).

5.4.
Bundles over
\mathcal{B}G

In this subsection we define for all
\nu\geq 0
a bundle map

\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{B}G
; the fibre of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}

can be identified with

\coprod_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},

where
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}\subset\mathcal{Q}
is the subset of elements of norm
\nu
.
In the case
\nu=0
, the map
\mathfrak{p}_{0}
is just the identity of
\mathcal{B}G=\mathfrak{F}_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
,
and the fibre is a point, i.e. the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\mathbbl{1}}
.

In the next section we will investigate the rational cohomology of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
using
the Leray spectral sequence associated with the filtration

F_{\bullet}=F_{\bullet}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
: the first page of this spectral sequence
contains the
relative
cohomology groups
H^{*}(F_{\nu},F_{\nu-1})
,
rather than the cohomology groups of the strata
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}
.
To acquire information about these relative cohomology groups, we introduce in this subsection certain subspaces
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}=F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, for
\nu\geq 0
.
We will prove, between this subsection and the next section, that

F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\subset F_{\nu}
,
that
F_{\nu-1}
is contained in the interior of
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}
when the latter is regarded as
a subspace of
F_{\nu}
, and that the inclusion
F_{\nu-1}\subset F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}
is a
homotopy equivalence.
In particular, after setting
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}:=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\cap F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}
,
we will obtain in Lemma
6.2
an isomorphism

H^{*}(F_{\nu},F_{\nu-1})\cong H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}).

In this subsection we will prove that
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
exhibits
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu})
as a couple of bundles over
\mathcal{B}G
,
with fibre a suitable couple of spaces

\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}\right)=\left(\coprod_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\coprod_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\right);

the computation of the rational cohomology
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu};\mathbb{Q})
will then be possible using the Serre spectral sequence associated with
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
.

Definition 5.20
.

Recall Definition
5.9
. Denote by
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
the
closed neighbourhood of
\partial\mathcal{R}
in
\mathcal{R}=[0,1]^{2}
given by

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}\setminus\left(\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\right)
,
where
\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
is the image of
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}

along the homothety centred at
z_{\mathrm{c}}
of rescaling factor
\frac{1}{2}
;
in other words,
\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
is the open square
of side length
\frac{1}{2}
centred at
z_{\mathrm{c}}
.

The identity of
\mathbb{C}
induces a map

(\Id{C})_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial;\mathcal{Q},G)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G).

Recall that
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)
has a filtration by subspaces
F_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)
for
\nu\geq-1
;
we define
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
as the intersection

F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G):=(\Id{C})_{*}^{-1}\left(F_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)\right)\ \cap\ F_{\nu+1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G).

In particular, we have
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{-1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)=\emptyset
.
Roughly speaking, for
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
,
we can use Notation
2.2
,
and suppose without loss of generality that
\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{l^{\prime}}\right\}=P\cap(1/4,3/4)^{2}
for some
0\leq l^{\prime}\leq l
;
if
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
is an admissible generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(P)
,
then
\mathfrak{c}
belongs to
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
if the following hold:

•

\sum_{i=1}^{l}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq\nu+1
, that is,
\mathfrak{c}\in F_{\nu+1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
;

•

\sum_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq\nu
.

Another characterisation is the following:
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
is the preimage of the space
F_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
along the
restricted map
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}(-,2)\colon F_{\nu+1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, see Definition
5.6
. Note that, in fact,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}(-,2)
restricts to a self-map of
F_{\nu+1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.
By construction, we have inclusions

F_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset F_{\nu+1}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G).

Notation 5.21
.

For a subspace
X\subseteq\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
and
\nu\geq-1
we denote by
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}X

the intersection
X\cap F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.
For
\nu\geq 0
we denote by
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}X
the intersection
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}X\cap F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}X
.

Example 5.22
.

Let
a\in\mathcal{Q}
and let
X=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
; the inclusion of nice couples
(\mathring{\mathcal{R}},\emptyset)\subset(\mathcal{R},\partial)
induces an inclusion of spaces
X\subset\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
; let
\nu=N(a)\geq 0
, and note that
F_{\nu-1}X=\emptyset
and, hence,

X=F_{\nu}X=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}X
.
The space
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}X
contains all configurations
\mathfrak{c}\in X
such that, using Notation
2.2
,
P

intersects non-trivially
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
: in fact the condition
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})
implies that the monodromy

\psi
attains values of norm
\geq 1
(i.e. different from
\mathbbl{1}\in\mathcal{Q}
)
around all points of
P
.
The space
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}X
coincides with
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}X
in this case, and by abuse of notation we will also
write

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}=\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}.

Example 5.23
.

Let
X=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
; then
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{-1}X=\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{0}X=\emptyset
and
\mathfrak{F}_{0}X=\mathcal{B}G
;
hence the identity of
\mathcal{B}G

can be regarded as a pair of bundles

\mathfrak{p}_{0}\colon\left(\,\mathfrak{F}_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\,,\,\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{0}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\,\right)\to\mathcal{B}G

with fibre the couple
(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\mathbbl{1}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\mathbbl{1}})=(\left\{(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1})\right\},\emptyset)
.
In the rest of the subsection we generalise this example to the other strata of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.

Fix in the following
\nu\geq 1
, let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
and use Notation
2.2
.
Let
\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{k}
be embedded arcs in
\mathbb{C}
joining
*
with the points
z_{1},\dots,z_{k}
of
P
, intersecting pairwise only at the endpoint
*
, and such that
\zeta_{i}\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
for
l+1\leq i\leq k
.
Recall that, since
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, the point
0
belongs to
P
; we use the convention that
z_{k}=0
. Let
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
be the admissible generating set of
\mathfrak{G}(P)
obtained by replacing each
\zeta_{i}
by a loop contained in a small neighbourhood of
\zeta_{i}
and spinning clockwise only around
z_{i}
.

We define a new configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}G=\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R};G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
as follows:

•

P^{\prime}
is the intersection
P\cap\partial\mathcal{R}
;

•

\psi^{\prime}
sends, for
l+1\leq i\leq k-1
, the generator
f_{i}
to
\varphi(f_{i})\in G
,
and it sends
f_{k}
to the unique element
\psi^{\prime}(f_{k})\in G
such that the resulting configuration

\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
satisfies
\omega(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})=\mathbbl{1}\in G
.

Note that
G
is treated as a complete PMQ when defining
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
. See Figure
9
.

\bullet
g_{6}
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
a_{1}
g_{4}
a_{2}
\bullet
\bullet
g_{5}
a_{3}
*
\bullet
g^{\prime}_{3}
\bullet
g^{\prime}_{1}
\bullet
g^{\prime}_{2}

Figure 9.
The configuration
\mathfrak{c}
from Figure
7
belongs to
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}
, where
\nu=N(a_{1})+N(a_{2})+N(a_{3})
, because the two points
z_{1}
and
z_{3}
lie in
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
. On the right, the image of
\mathfrak{c}
along
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
; note that the loop with label
g_{4}
on the left is
not
constructed using an arc contained in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
; as a consequence the monodromy on right around the “same” loop is changed by conjugation:
we have in fact
g^{\prime}_{1}=g_{4}^{\mathfrak{e}(a_{2})\left(\mathfrak{e}(a_{3})^{g_{5}^{-1}}\right)}
,
g^{\prime}_{2}=g_{5}
and

g^{\prime}_{3}=g_{6}\mathfrak{e}(a_{1})\mathfrak{e}(a_{2})g_{5}\left(\mathfrak{e}(a_{3})^{g_{5}^{-1}}\right)
.

Definition 5.24
.

The previous assignment
\mathfrak{c}\mapsto\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
defines a map of sets
