\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{B}G.

To check that the previous is a good definition, we need to verify that the choice of the arcs
\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{k}

is not relevant in computing
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{c})
. The generator
f_{i}
is uniquely defined up to conjugation
by a power of the element
[\gamma]\in\mathfrak{G}(P)
represented by a loop
\gamma
spinning clockwise around
\mathcal{R}
.
It follows that
\psi(f_{i})
is well-defined, as an element of
G
, up to conjugation by a power of
\psi([\gamma])
,
i.e. up to conjugation by a power of
\omega(\mathfrak{c})=\mathbbl{1}\in G
: here we use that the total monodromy attains constantly the value

\mathbbl{1}\in G
on configurations of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
; this shows that
\psi(f_{i})\in G
is well-defined
for
1\leq i\leq k-1
, and
\psi(f_{k})
is also uniquely determined by the values
\psi(f_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq k-1

and by its characterising property. Therefore,
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{c})
is well-defined.

We next check that
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{B}G
is continuous. Roughly speaking,
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
splits a configuration in
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
in two parts, the part supported on
\partial\mathcal{R}
and the part supported on
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
, and it pushes all points in the second part to
0
, thus giving rise to a new configuration supported only on
\partial\mathcal{R}
. Continuity of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
depends on the fact that if we perturb a configuration
staying inside the stratum
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, then no point in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
can collide with
\partial\mathcal{R}
, and vice versa no point in
\partial\mathcal{R}
can move in the interior, as this would let the internal total norm jump (down and up, respectively). Here it is important that, working with
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, the local monodromy of a point lying in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
is required to have positive norm.

Formally, let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
and let
\underline{U}
be an adapted covering of
P
: in particular,
we have
z_{i}\in U_{i}\subset\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
for all
1\leq i\leq l
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{c})\in\mathcal{B}G
, and let
\underline{U}^{\prime}
be the restricted,
adapted covering of
P^{\prime}
, i.e.
\underline{U}^{\prime}=(U_{l+1},\dots,U_{k})
.
Then
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
sends the intersection
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})\cap\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
inside

\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\underline{U}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{B}G
: this essentially follows from the observation that, for
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}=(\tilde{P},\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\varphi})\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})\cap\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, we have
\tilde{P}\subset U_{1}\cup\dots\cup U_{l}\cup\left(\underline{U}^{\prime}\cap\partial\mathcal{R}\right)
.

Notation 5.25
.

Let
\nu\geq 0
, let
\mathbb{T}\subset\mathbb{C}
be a contractible subspace containing
*
, let

\mathcal{X}\subset\mathring{\mathbb{T}}
be a semialgebraic subspace
and let
X
be a subspace of
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Q})
.
We denote
X_{\nu}=\coprod_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\left(X\cap\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathcal{X};\mathcal{Q})_{a}\right)
.

Proposition 5.26
.

The map
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{B}G
is a bundle map with fibre

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}
.
The restricted map
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\to\mathcal{B}G
is also a bundle map with fibre

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}
.
The two bundles admit compatible local trivialisations, i.e. they form a couple of bundles.

Proof.

Choose a small
closed
interval
J\subset(0,1)\times\left\{0\right\}\subset\partial\mathcal{R}
, and choose
an arc
\zeta_{J}
joining
*
with the midpoint
of
J
. Let
\mathbb{T}
be the union
\mathbb{T}=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\cup J\cup\zeta_{J}\subset\mathbb{C}
, and note that
\mathbb{T}
is contractible and contains

\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
in its interior. We can define a map of sets

\mathfrak{i}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\cap\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R}\setminus J,\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;\mathcal{Q},G)\to\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu},

in the spirit of
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.15]
.
To define this map, let
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\cap\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R}\setminus J,\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;\mathcal{Q},G)
: using Notation
2.2
,
this means that
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
and the support
P
of
\mathfrak{c}
does not intersect
J
.
The inclusion
\mathbb{T}\setminus P\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus P
gives rise to an inclusion of groups
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathbb{T}}(P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}})\subset\mathfrak{G}(P)

and, by restriction, an inclusion of PMQs
\mathfrak{Q}^{\mathbb{T}}_{(\mathring{\mathcal{R}},\emptyset)}(P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}})\subset\mathfrak{Q}_{(\mathcal{R},\partial)}(P)
.
We define
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{c})
to be the configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
, where
P^{\prime}=P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
and
\psi^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{Q}^{\mathbb{T}}_{(\mathring{\mathcal{R}},\emptyset)}(P^{\prime})\to\mathcal{Q}

is the composition of the above inclusion with
\psi\colon\mathfrak{Q}_{(\mathcal{R},\partial)}(P)\to\mathcal{Q}
.

To show that
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathbb{T}}^{\mathbb{C}}
is continuous at
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\cap\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R}\setminus J,\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;\mathcal{Q},G)
,
let
\underline{U}
be an adapted covering of
P
with
\underline{U}\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus J
, let
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{c})
as above, and
let
\underline{U}^{\prime}
be the restriction of
\underline{U}
to
P^{\prime}\subset P
, i.e.
\underline{U}^{\prime}
consists of those components of
\underline{U}
that intersect
non-trivially
P^{\prime}
; equivalently,
\underline{U}^{\prime}
consists of those components of
\underline{U}
that are contained in
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
.

We claim that
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}
sends
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})\cap\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
inside

\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\underline{U}^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}
; since every small enough adapted
covering
\underline{U}^{\prime}
of
P^{\prime}
with respect to
(\mathring{\mathcal{R}},\emptyset)
can be extended to an
adapted covering
\underline{U}
of
P
with respect to
(\mathcal{R}\setminus J,\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J)
,
the claim suffices to prove continuity of
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

For the claim, let
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}=(\hat{P},\hat{\psi},\hat{\varphi})\in\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c},\underline{U})\cap\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
;
fix an admissible generating set
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}

of
\mathfrak{G}(P)
extending an admissible generating set
f_{1},\dots,f_{l}
of
\mathfrak{G}^{\mathbb{T}}(P^{\prime})
, and regard

f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
as elements of
\mathfrak{G}(\hat{P})
by the inclusion
\mathfrak{G}(P)\cong\mathfrak{G}(\underline{U})\subset\mathfrak{G}(\hat{P})
.
Let
\hat{P}_{i}=\hat{P}\cap U_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
, and write
\hat{P}_{i}=\left\{z_{i,1},\dots,z_{i,k_{i}}\right\}
.
Choose an admissible generating set
(\hat{f}_{i,j})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq k_{i}}
of
\mathfrak{G}(\hat{P})
,
such that the equality
f_{i}=\hat{f}_{i,1}\cdot\dots\cdot\hat{f}_{i,k_{i}}
holds in
\mathfrak{G}(\hat{P})
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
The hypothesis on
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}
implies that for all
1\leq i\leq l
the product

\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,1})\dots\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,k_{i}})
is defined in
\mathcal{Q}
and is equal to
\psi(f_{i})
,
in particular
\sum_{j=1}^{k_{i}}N(\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,j}))=N(\psi(f_{i}))
. Summing over
1\leq i\leq l
,
and recalling that
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}\in\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
, we obtain the equality

\sum_{i=1}^{l}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{i}}N(\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,j}))=\nu
.
This implies that
\hat{P}
can
only intersect the open sets
U_{l+1},\dots,U_{k}
in points of
\partial\mathcal{R}
or in
inert
points for
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}
;
since
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}
has no inert point, we have that
\hat{P}
is contained in the union
\partial\mathcal{R}\cup\underline{U}^{\prime}
.
It follows that
\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\hat{\mathfrak{c}})
is supported on
\underline{U}^{\prime}
; the fact that

\hat{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
is contained in
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime},\underline{U}^{\prime})
follows now directly from the definition of
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}

and from the already mentioned equalities

\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,1})\cdot\dots\cdot\hat{\psi}(\hat{f}_{i,k_{i}})=\psi(f_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq l
.

Now note that the intersection
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\cap\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R}\setminus J,\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;\mathcal{Q},G)
is precisely the preimage along
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
of the open subspace
\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\subset\mathcal{B}G
. The product map
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}
gives a homeomorphism

\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}\ \colon\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}^{-1}(\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}})\cong\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}\times\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}.

Since the open sets
\mathrm{Hur}(\partial\mathcal{R}\setminus J;G)_{0;\mathbbl{1}}
form an open covering
of
\mathcal{B}G
, for varying
J
, we obtain that

\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
is a bundle map, i.e it admits local trivialisations. The fibre of the bundle
is homeomorphic to the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}
.

The local trivialisation
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}
restricts to a local trivialisation of
the restriction of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}
to
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)\subset\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
,
with restricted fibre
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}
.
∎

6.
Rational cohomology

In this section we assume that
\mathcal{Q}
is a finite,
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré PMQ and
G
is a finite group,
and compute the rational cohomology of
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
. Recall from
[
Bia23a
, Definition]
that a PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré
if
\mathcal{Q}
is locally finite and for all
a\in\mathcal{Q}
the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
is a

\mathbb{Q}
-homology manifold of some dimension: in this case
\mathcal{Q}
admits an intrinsic norm
h\colon\mathcal{Q}\to\mathbb{N}

and
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
is an orientable
\mathbb{Q}
-homology manifold of dimension
2h(a)
for all
a\in\mathcal{Q}
. See
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 9.7]
.

Our interest for the space
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
and its cohomology comes from Theorem
4.22

and Proposition
5.5
, relating
\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
to the topological monoid
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q})
.
Note that, for a fixed PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
, we are free to choose a group
G
completing
\mathcal{Q}
to a PMQ-group pair
(\mathcal{Q},G,\mathfrak{e},\mathfrak{r})
. If
\mathcal{Q}
is finite we can, for instance, take
G=\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})/\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{Q})
, which is a finite group; here
\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{Q})
denotes the kernel of the map
\rho\colon\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})\to\Aut_{\mathbf{PMQ}}(\mathcal{Q})^{op}
, giving the right action of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})
on
\mathcal{Q}
by conjugation: if
\mathcal{Q}
is finite, then
\Aut_{\mathbf{PMQ}}(\mathcal{Q})^{op}
is also finite and contains a subgroup isomorphic to
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Q})/\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{Q})
. See also
[
Bia21
, Lemma 2.13]
.
Thus, if we are given a
finite
,
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
,
we can complete
\mathcal{Q}
to a PMQ-group pair by adjoining a suitable finite group
G
.

Recall from
[
Bia21
, Definition 4.29]
that
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})\subset\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]
is defined as the subring of the
PMQ-group ring
\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]
consisting of the invariants under conjugation by
G
:

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})=\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]^{G}.

As a
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space,
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
is spanned by elements
[\![S]\!]=\sum_{a\in S}[\![a]\!]
,
for each conjugacy class
S\subset\mathcal{Q}
. In this section we consider

\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]^{G}
as a graded, associative ring, by putting the generator
[\![a]\!]
in degree
2h(a)
,
for all
a\in\mathcal{Q}
; similarly

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
is a graded ring with

[\![S]\!]
in degree
2h(a)
, for any
a\in S
. By
[
Bia21
, Lemma 4.31]
the ring

\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
is a commutative ring, hence by our choice of degrees it is a graded-commutative
ring, supported in even degrees. We will prove the following theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},G)
be a PMQ-group pair with
\mathcal{Q}
finite and
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré and with
G
finite.
Then there is an isomorphism of rings

H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G);\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q}).

In this entire section we use the abbreviation
\mathbb{B}=\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
.

6.1.
Two spectral sequence arguments

Since the space
\mathbb{B}
is equipped with a filtration by subspaces
F_{\nu}\mathbb{B}
,
we can compute
H^{*}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
by the associated Leray spectral sequence, whose first page reads

E_{1}^{p,\nu}=H^{p+\nu}(F_{\nu}\mathbb{B},F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B})
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
\nu\geq 0
the inclusion
F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}\subset F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}
is a homotopy equivalence.
Moreover we have cohomology isomorphisms

H^{*}(F_{\nu}\mathbb{B},F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})\cong H^{*}(F_{\nu}\mathbb{B},F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})\cong H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}).

Proof.

Recall Definitions
5.6
and
5.7
.
For
\mathfrak{c}\in F_{\nu}\mathbb{B}
let
\mathfrak{W}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{c})
be the supremum in
[1/2,1]
of all
1/2<\varepsilon<1

for which
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}(\mathfrak{c},1/\varepsilon)\in F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}
. We define a homotopy

H^{\mathbb{B}}_{\nu}\colon F_{\nu}\mathbb{B}\times[0,1]\to F_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\quad(\mathfrak{c},s)\mapsto\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c},1-s+\frac{s}{\mathfrak{W}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{c})}\right).

The homotopy
H^{\mathbb{B}}_{\nu}
restricts to a deformation retraction of
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}

onto
F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}
, whence the first cohomology isomorphism follows.
The second cohomology isomorphism follows from excision: in fact,

F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}=(\mathfrak{W}_{\nu})^{-1}(1)

and the open set
(\mathfrak{W}_{\nu})^{-1}((1/2,1])
is contained in
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}(\mathbb{B})
,
so we can apply excision.
∎

We can now focus on the relative cohomology groups
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
.

Proposition 6.3
.

For
\nu\geq 0
, the cohomology groups
H^{*}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
are concentrated in degree
*=2\nu
;
the group
H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})_{2\nu}
, i.e.
the degree-
2\nu
part of
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
.

Proof.

We use the Serre spectral sequence
\mathcal{E}(\nu)
associated with the couple of bundles

\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B})\to\mathcal{B}G
: its second page reads

\mathcal{E}(\nu)_{2}^{p,q}=H^{p}\left(\mathcal{B}G;H^{q}\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu};\mathbb{Q}\right)\right).

The first step is to compute
H^{*}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a};\mathbb{Q})
for
a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
,
and the argument for this will be similar to the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
Consider the closed unit square
\mathcal{R}
and
define
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}:=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\setminus\mathrm{Hur}((\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}});\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
,
and
\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}:=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\setminus\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
.
The subspace
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
contains configurations
whose support intersects
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
(see Definition
5.20
), whereas
\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\subset\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
contains configurations whose support intersects
\partial\mathcal{R}
.

Recall Definition
5.6
: for all
s\geq 1
the map
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}(-,s)\colon\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}

is a lax endomorphism of the nice couple
(\mathcal{R},\emptyset)
; if we consider

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
as a connected component of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{+})
, under the inclusion
\mathcal{Q}\subset\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
,
we obtain by functoriality a homotopy

\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times[1,\infty)\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}.

For
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
denote by
\mathfrak{W}_{a}(\mathfrak{c})
the supremum in

[1/2,1]
of all
1/2<\varepsilon<1
for which
\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}(\mathfrak{c},1/\varepsilon)\in\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
.
We define a homotopy

H^{\mathbb{B}}_{a}\colon\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\quad\quad(\mathfrak{c},s)\mapsto\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c}\,,\,1-s+\frac{s}{\mathfrak{W}_{a}(\mathfrak{c})}\right).

The homotopy
H^{\mathbb{B}}_{a}
restricts to a deformation retraction of
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}

onto
\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
; moreover the subspace
\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
is contained in the interior
of
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
, and

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\setminus\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}=\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}.

We thus obtain cohomology isomorphisms

\begin{split}H^{*}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a};\mathbb{Q})\cong&H^{*}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a};\mathbb{Q})\\[5.69046pt]
\cong&H^{*}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a};\mathbb{Q}).\end{split}

Since
\mathcal{Q}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré, as a consequence of
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 9.5, Proposition 9.7]
the cohomology

H^{*}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a};\mathbb{Q})
vanishes in degrees
*\neq 2\nu=2h(a)
,
and it is equal to
\mathbb{Q}
in degree
*=2\nu
. Going back to the Serre spectral sequence,
the group
\mathcal{E}(\nu)_{2}^{p,q}
vanishes for
q\neq 2\nu
, and

\mathcal{E}(\nu)_{2}^{p,2\nu}
is equal to the twisted cohomology group

H^{p}\left(\mathcal{B}G;\oplus_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathbb{Q}\right)
; this already shows that the spectral sequence
collapses on its second page. Moreover, since
G
is a finite group and we are considering
twisted cohomology with coefficients in a
G
-representation over
\mathbb{Q}
, all cohomology
groups except possibly
H^{0}
vanish, i.e. the entire page
\mathcal{E}(\nu)_{2}
vanishes
except possibly
\mathcal{E}(\nu)_{2}^{0,2\nu}=H^{0}\left(\mathcal{B}G;\bigoplus_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathbb{Q}\right)
.

The action of
G
on
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathbb{Q}
is the
\mathbb{Q}
-linearisation of the action of
G

on the set
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
by conjugation; the invariants of the
action of
G
on
\bigoplus_{a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}}\mathbb{Q}

are therefore the
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space spanned by conjugacy classes of
\mathcal{Q}
of norm
\nu
: this vector
space is isomorphic to the degree-
2\nu
part of
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
.
∎

Proposition
6.3
implies that the
E_{1}
-page of the Leray spectral sequence
associated with the filtered space
\mathbb{B}
is supported on the main diagonal, i.e.
E_{1}^{p,\nu}=0

whenever
p\neq\nu
. This implies that the spectral sequence collapses on its first page
and, thus, we obtain an isomorphism of graded
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces

H^{*}(\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G);\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q}).

It will be convenient to specify a particular isomorphism of graded
\mathbb{Q}
-vector spaces.
Recall that, since
\mathcal{Q}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré, it is locally finite and coconnected;
in particular, for all
\nu\geq 0
and for all
b\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
there is a canonical fundamental class

[\mathrm{Arr}(\mathcal{Q})_{b},\mathrm{NAdm}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}]\in H_{2\nu}\left(|\mathrm{Arr}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}|,|\mathrm{NAdm}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}|;\mathbb{Q}\right),

see
[
Bia21
, Definition 6.25]
. Recall also the map of pairs

\upsilon=\upsilon_{b}\colon\left(|\mathrm{Arr}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}|,|\mathrm{NAdm}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}|\right)\to\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}\right);

by
[
Bia23a
, Lemma 8.23]

\upsilon
is a continuous bijection, and the hypothesis
that
\mathcal{Q}
is locally finite implies that
|\mathrm{Arr}(\mathcal{Q})_{b}|
is compact, hence

\upsilon_{b}
is a homeomorphism. We thus obtain a fundamental class

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}]\in H_{2\nu}\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\mathbb{Q}\right).

Using the homotopy equivalences of pairs

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b})\simeq(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b})

and excision we obtain a fundamental class

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}]\in H_{2\nu}\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\mathbb{Q}\right).

Notation 6.4
.

Let
[\![S]\!]\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})_{2\nu}
be the generator corresponding to the conjugacy class
S\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
.
We regard
[\![S]\!]
as the (unique) cohomology class

[\![S]\!]\in H^{2\nu}\left(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu};\mathbb{Q}\right)

satisfying the following property: for all
b\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
,
the Kronecker pairing of
[\![S]\!]
with the fundamental homology class

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}]

is
1
if
b\in S
and is
0
if
b\notin S
.

Note that
[\![S]\!]
is invariant under the action of
G
by conjugation, hence
[\![S]\!]
corresponds
to a cohomology class in
H^{2\nu}\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}\right)
,
which we also denote
[\![S]\!]
. Finally, we use the canonical isomorphisms

H^{2\nu}\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}\right)\overset{\cong}{\leftarrow}H^{2\nu}\left(F_{\nu}\mathbb{B},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}\right)\overset{\cong}{\to}H^{2\nu}\left(F_{\nu}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}\right)\overset{\cong}{\leftarrow}H^{2\nu}\left(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q}\right)

to regard
[\![S]\!]
as a cohomology class in
H^{2\nu}(\mathbb{B}(\mathcal{Q}_{+},G);\mathbb{Q})
.

6.2.
A strategy to compute the cup product

We fix
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\geq 0
throughout the rest of the section; our aim is to compute the cup product

H^{2\nu}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})\to H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
.
We fix
[\![S]\!]\in H^{2\nu}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
and
[\![S^{\prime}]\!]\in H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
:
our aim is to express the cup product
[\![S]\!]\smile[\![S^{\prime}]\!]\in H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
as a linear combination
of generators
[\![T]\!]
, for
T
varying among conjugacy classes of
\mathcal{Q}
contained in
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
.

The restriction map
H^{*}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})\to H^{*}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})

is an isomorphism in degrees
*\leq 2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}
:
therefore, it suffices to compute the cup product
H^{2\nu}\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}\to H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}
for the space

F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}
. In the rest of the subsection we use the abbreviation
F_{\bullet}=F_{\bullet}\mathbb{B}
.

The argument to compute the cup product on
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
is based on certain
subspaces
F_{\mathrm{lr}}
,
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
of
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
; there are
inclusions
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{lr}}
as well as
F_{\nu-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\nu^{\prime}-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, and we will prove that the last two
inclusions are, in fact, homotopy equivalences.
Postponing the actual definition of
F_{\mathrm{lr}}
,
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, we introduce some notation.

Notation 6.5
.

We introduce several subspaces of
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
:

F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}=F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\cup F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\quad\quad\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus F_{\mathrm{lr}};

\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\quad\quad\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\quad\quad\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}};

\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\quad\quad\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\quad\quad\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}.

There is the following square of inclusions of subspaces, where both horizontal arrows are inclusions of a closed subspace of
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

in the interior of a larger subspace of
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathrm{lr}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}.}

This implies that the inclusions of couples

(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})\subset(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})

and
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})\subset(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})

satisfy excision.
Finally, the bundle projection
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
exhibits also the subspaces

\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
,
\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
,
\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
,
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
,

\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
,
\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
as bundles over
\mathcal{B}G
, with suitable fibres: local trivialisations for these bundles can be obtained by restricting the local trivialisations of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
given in the proof of Proposition
5.26
.

The previous technical results will allow us to write two commutative diagrams of cohomology groups,
where we understand
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients for cohomology.
We state the two diagrams as propositions for future reference.

Proposition 6.6
.

There is a commutative diagram of cohomology groups as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}

We will consider
[\![S]\!]\otimes[\![S^{\prime}]\!]
as an element in
H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
in the previous diagram, and compute explicitly the image of the cup product
[\![S]\!]\smile[\![S^{\prime}]\!]
in
H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})\cong H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})
.

Proposition 6.7
.

There is a commutative diagram of cohomology groups as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

We will compute the image along the natural map
\theta
of
[\![S]\!]\smile[\![S^{\prime}]\!]
,
and identify it with the class
[\![S]\!]\cdot[\![S^{\prime}]\!]\in\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})_{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}
.

6.3.
Proof of Propositions
6.6
and
6.7

Definition 6.8
.

Recall Definition
5.20
. We let
z_{\mathrm{c,l}}=\frac{2}{7}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}

and
z_{\mathrm{c,r}}=\frac{5}{7}+\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2}
; note that the homothety centred at
z_{\mathrm{c,l}}

with rescaling factor
1/4
maps
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
to the open square
(1/4,3/8)\times(7/16,9/16)
,
i.e.
(1/4,3/8)\times(7/16,9/16)=\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
;
similarly
(5/8,3/4)\times(7/16,9/16)=\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,r}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
. See Figure
10
.

For
\bullet=\mathrm{l},\mathrm{r}
, we define a subspace
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{R}
of
\mathcal{R}
by

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{R}:=\mathcal{R}\setminus(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c},\bullet}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
. The identity of
\mathbb{C}
is a map of nice couples

(\mathcal{R},\partial\mathcal{R})\to(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{R})
,
giving rise to a map
(\Id{C})_{*}\colon F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}=F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\mathbb{B}\to\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)
.
Recall Definition
5.9
: we define
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\subset F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
as the preimage along
(\Id{C})_{*}
of

F_{\nu-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)
and, respectively,

F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\subset F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
as the preimage along
(\Id{C})_{*}
of

F_{\nu^{\prime}-1}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R};\mathcal{Q},G)
.

z_{\mathrm{c,l}}
z_{\mathrm{c,r}}

Figure 10.
The complement of the left (respectively, right) white square is
\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}

(respectively,
\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
), whereas the total grey area is
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
; the dotted vertical
line splits the total grey area into
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{r}}
.

Roughly speaking, a configuration
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)\in F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
lies in
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
if the sum of the norms of
the values of the monodromy
\psi
around points of
P
lying in the open square
(1/4,3/8)\times(7/16,9/16)

does not exceed
\nu-1
; similarly for
F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, referring to the open square
(5/8,3/4)\times(7/16,9/16)

and replacing the threshold
\nu-1
with
\nu^{\prime}-1
.
To keep the notation simple, we avoid adding the indices
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
to
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
.
Note that

F_{\nu-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\nu^{\prime}-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
.

Lemma 6.9
.

The inclusions

F_{\nu-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
and
F_{\nu^{\prime}-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
are homotopy equivalences.

Proof.

We focus on the inclusion
F_{\nu-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, the other one being analogous.
Recall Definition
5.6
and the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
For
\mathfrak{c}\in F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
we denote by
\mathfrak{W}_{\mathrm{l},\nu}(\mathfrak{c})
the supremum
in
[1/8,1]
of all
1/8<\varepsilon<1

for which
\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}\right)_{*}(\mathfrak{c},1/\varepsilon)\in F_{\nu-1}\mathbb{B}
. We define a homotopy

H^{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{l},\nu}\colon F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\times[0,1]\to F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\quad\quad(\mathfrak{c},s)\mapsto\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{B}}_{z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}\right)_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c}\,,\,1-s+\frac{s}{\mathfrak{W}_{\mathrm{l},\nu}(\mathfrak{c})}\right).

The homotopy
H^{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathrm{l},\nu}
restricts to a deformation retraction of
F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}

onto
F_{\nu-1}
.
∎

Lemma
6.9
implies the top left isomorphism in Proposition
6.6
.

Lemma 6.10
.

The space
F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}
is contained in the union

F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\cup F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{c}\in F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}
, use Notation
2.2
, and for
\bullet=\mathrm{l,r}
denote by

P_{\bullet}\subset P
the intersection of
P
with the open square

(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c},\bullet}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that there are indices
0\leq l^{\prime}\leq l^{\prime\prime}\leq l
such that
P_{\mathrm{l}}=\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{l^{\prime}}\right\}

and
P_{\mathrm{r}}=\left\{z_{l^{\prime}+1},\dots,z_{l^{\prime\prime}}\right\}
. Let
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}
be an admissible generating set. Then the hypothesis
\mathfrak{c}\in F^{\mathrm{fat}}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}
, together with the fact that
(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})

and
(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,r}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
are disjoint and contained in
(\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
, implies the inequality

\sum_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}N(\psi(f_{i}))+\sum_{i=l^{\prime}+1}^{l^{\prime\prime}}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1.

It follows that at least one of the following two inequalities holds

\sum_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq\nu-1;\quad\quad\sum_{i=l^{\prime}+1}^{l^{\prime\prime}}N(\psi(f_{i}))\leq\nu^{\prime}-1;

the first inequality implies
\mathfrak{c}\in F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, the second implies
\mathfrak{c}\in F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
.
∎

Notation 6.11
.

We introduce several subspaces of
\mathcal{R}
, see Figure
10
:

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}=[0,1/2]\times[0,1],\quad\quad\quad\quad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{r}}=[1/2,1]\times[0,1],

\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}=(0,1/2)\times(0,1),\quad\quad\quad\quad\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{r}}=(1/2,1)\times(0,1),

\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}},\quad\quad\quad\quad\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{r}}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{r}}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}},

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}\setminus\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\right),\quad\quad\quad\quad\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{l}}\setminus\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\right),

\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}\setminus\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\right),\quad\quad\quad\quad\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}=\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}\setminus\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\right).

Definition 6.12
.

Recall Definition
5.9
.
The identity of
\mathbb{C}
induces maps of nice couples
(\mathcal{R};\partial)\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}})
and

(\mathcal{R};\partial)\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}})
.
We define
F_{\mathrm{lr}}\subset F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
as the subspace of configurations
\mathfrak{c}
such that at least one of the following conditions holds:

•

(\Id{C})_{*}(\mathfrak{c})\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
has norm
\leq\nu-1
;

•

(\Id{C})_{*}(\mathfrak{c})\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+},G)
has norm
\leq\nu^{\prime}-1
.

Roughly speaking, the complement
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
of
F_{\mathrm{lr}}
in
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
contains those configurations

\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)
such that
P\subset\mathring{\mathcal{R}}\setminus\left\{1/2\right\}\times(0,1)
, the sum of the norms of the values
of
\psi
around points of
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}
is equal to
\nu
, and the sum of the norms of the values
of
\psi
around points of
P\cap\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}}
is equal to
\nu^{\prime}
.

Lemma 6.13
.

The space
F_{\mathrm{lr}}
is contained in the interior of
F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
, considered as subspace of
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Given
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi,\varphi)\in F_{\mathrm{lr}}
, it suffices to note that for any adapted covering
\underline{U}
of
P
with the sets

U_{i}
of diameter at most
1/8
, the restricted normal neighbourhood
\mathfrak{U}(\mathfrak{c};\underline{U})\cap F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

is contained in
F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}
.
∎

Lemma
6.13
implies, together with the inclusion
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}\subset F_{\mathrm{lr}}
,
that the couple
(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
satisfies excision with respect to the subspaces

F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}
and
F_{\mathrm{lr}}
, i.e. the two bottom right vertical isomorphisms in Proposition
6.6
hold.
This concludes the proof of Proposition
6.6
.

In the same way, the two bottom vertical isomorphisms of Proposition
6.7
follow from excision
of the subspace
F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}-1}
, whereas the top diagonal isomorphism follows from the computation
of
H^{*}(\mathbb{B};\mathbb{Q})
using the Leray spectral sequence. This concludes also the proof of Proposition
6.7
.

6.4.
Conclusion of the proof of Theorem
6.1

As remarked already,
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
exhibits
all subspaces of
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
occurring in the bottom rows of Propositions
6.6
and
6.7
as bundles over
\mathcal{B}G
: the proof of Proposition
5.26
provides local trivialisations also for these bundles. Our next aim is to compute the cohomology groups of these bundles and of the couples of bundles they form.

Definition 6.14
.

For
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\geq 0
we denote by
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}\times\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}
the subset of couples

(a,b)
for which the product
ab
is defined in
\mathcal{Q}
.
We set

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:=\coprod_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}.

We regard
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
as a subspace of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
by regarding
each product
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}
as a subspace of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{ab}
under the inclusion given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,1}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{ab}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{ab}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,1}})^{-1}}

The generic fibre of the bundle
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
is homeomorphic to the space
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
. To see this, note that the same argument of the proof of Proposition
5.26

identifies the fibre of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\colon\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
with the subspace of

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}}\cup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

containing configurations
\mathfrak{c}
with the following properties:

•

\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}
sends
\mathfrak{c}
to a configuration in

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})
with total monodromy in
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
;

•

\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}
sends
\mathfrak{c}
to a configuration in

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})
with total monodromy in
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}
;

•

the product of the elements
\omega(\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}(\mathfrak{c}))
and

\omega(\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}(\mathfrak{c}))
is defined in
\mathcal{Q}
.

We can further identify the fibres of the following restrictions of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
:

•

the fibre of
\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
is identified with
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, as already mentioned;

•

the fibre of
\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
is identified with the intersection of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
with the product
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}\times\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu^{\prime}}
, i.e. with the space

\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:=\coprod_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},

where
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}:=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\setminus\mathrm{Hur}(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
;

•

the fibre of
\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
is identified with the intersection of
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
with the product
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu}\times\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu^{\prime}}
, i.e. with the space

\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:=\coprod_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}\times\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},

where
\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}:=\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}\setminus\mathrm{Hur}(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,r}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}
;

•

the fibre of
\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{B}G
is identified with the union

\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}:=\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cup\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.

By using functoriality with respect to suitable semialgebraic homeomorphisms of
\mathbb{C}
,
we can identify the couples of spaces

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a})\cong(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a});

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b})\cong(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}).

Notation 6.15
.

For
d\geq 0
we denote by

\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[d]
the graded
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space concentrated in degree
d
,
with basis the set
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.

For
d,d^{\prime}\geq 0
we denote by
\mu_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\colon\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[d]\otimes\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[d^{\prime}]\to\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[d+d^{\prime}]
the pairing given
by
(a,b)\otimes(a,b)\mapsto(a,b)
for
(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
and
(a,b)\otimes(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\mapsto 0

for
(a,b)\neq(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.

Consider the bottom row of Proposition
6.6
: all groups involved are cohomology groups of couples of bundles
over
\mathcal{B}G
.
The cup product on fibres reads as follows, where we simplify the notation by writing
\mathrm{Hur}
for
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu]\otimes\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu^{\prime}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}

Notation 6.16
.

For
a\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
we denote by
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{l},a}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a}
the unique configuration
supported on
\left\{z_{\mathrm{c,l}}\right\}
; similarly,
for
b\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}
we denote by
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b}
the unique configuration
supported on
\left\{z_{\mathrm{c,r}}\right\}
.

The three cohomology groups in the top row of the previous diagram have bases in bijection with the set
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
.
For instance,
H^{2\nu}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}))
has a basis
given by the cohomology duals of the homology classes

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}]
.
Here
[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\in H_{2\nu}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}};\mathbb{Q})
is the fundamental
homology class, and
[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}]\in H_{0}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b};\mathbb{Q})

denotes the “ground” homology class.

A similar description gives a basis for
H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
in bijection
with
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, whereas for
H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
we consider
the cohomology duals of the homology classes

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]
.
Taking
G
-invariants, we obtain an explicit computation of the bottom row of Proposition

6.6
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})\otimes H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu]\Big)^{G}\otimes\Big(\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu^{\prime}]\Big)^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}]\Big)^{G}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\smile}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
S\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
be a conjugacy class of
\mathcal{Q}
, and consider
[\![S]\!]
as a cohomology class in the group

\in H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})\cong H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\nu-1})\cong H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})
; the restriction of
[\![S]\!]

to
H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})

is the element

\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap S\times\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}}(a,b)\in\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu].

Similarly, for a conjugacy class
S^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}
, the class
[\![S^{\prime}]\!]\in H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
restricts to

\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}\times S^{\prime}}(a,b)\in\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu^{\prime}]\cong H^{2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}).

Proof.

We focus on the first part of the statement, the second being analogous.
Fix
(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
; the couple of spaces
(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})\times\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}

can be embedded into the couple of bundles
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})

as part of the fibre over the basepoint of
\mathcal{B}G
.
We consider the homology class

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}]\in H_{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q});

such classes generate the group
H_{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})
,
so in order to identify the restriction of
[\![S]\!]\in H^{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\mathbb{Q})
to

H^{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})
,
it suffices to compute the Kronecker pairing of the restricted
[\![S]\!]
with all homology classes of the form

[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}]
.
Let
\mathfrak{j}
be the composite

\mathfrak{j}\colon(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})\times\left\{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}\right\}\hookrightarrow(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}})\subset(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}});

then we can also consider the homology class
\mathfrak{j}_{*}([\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}])
in

H_{2\nu}(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\mathbb{Q})
, and compute its Kronecker pairing with
[\![S]\!]
.

Fix a homotopy
\mathcal{H}\colon\mathbb{C}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}
with the following properties:

•

for all
0\leq s\leq 1
,
\mathcal{H}(-,s)
is a semialgebraic self-map of
\mathbb{C}
fixing
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
pointwise, and sending
\partial_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathcal{R}
into itself;

•

\mathcal{H}(-,0)
is the identity of
\mathbb{C}
;

•

\mathcal{H}(-,1)

restricts to a homeomorphism of couples
(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}})\overset{\cong}{\to}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
,
and it sends
z_{\mathrm{c,r}}\mapsto z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
.

Then
\mathcal{H}
induces a homotopy of maps of couples

\mathcal{H}_{*}\colon(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})\times[0,1]\to(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}),

and composing this homotopy with
\mathfrak{j}
we obtain a homotopy of maps of couples

H=\mathcal{H}\circ(\mathfrak{j}\times\Id)\colon(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})\times\left\{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}\right\}\times[0,1]\to(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}}).

Since
H(-,0)=\mathfrak{j}
, we have

\mathfrak{j}_{*}([\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}])=H(-,1)_{*}([\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}]),

so we can focus on the Kronecker pairing of the latter homology class with
[\![S]\!]
.

Now note that, by construction,

H(-,1)
can be considered as a map of couples

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})\times\left\{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}\right\}\to(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu})\subset(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}});

more precisely

H(-,1)
restricts to a homeomorphism of

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})\times\left\{\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}\right\}
onto

(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}})
, where the latter couple is considered as part of the fibre
of
\mathfrak{p}_{\nu}\colon(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu})\to\mathcal{B}G
over the unique configuration

\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},\mathfrak{e}(b)}=(P,\psi)
satisfying the following properties:

•

\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},\mathfrak{e}(b)}
is supported on the set
P=\left\{0,z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}\right\}
;

•

if
\gamma
is a simple loop in
\mathbb{S}_{0,\infty}
spinning clockwise around
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
,
then
\psi([\gamma])=\mathfrak{e}(b)\in G
.

It follows that
H(-,1)_{*}([\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathfrak{c}_{\mathrm{r},b}])

is the image of the homology class
[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\in H_{2\nu}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu})

under the inclusion
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu})\subset(F_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},F_{\mathrm{l}}^{\mathrm{fat}})
;
the Kronecker pairing of the latter class with
[\![S]\!]
is 1 if and only if
a
belongs to
S
.
∎

Lemma
6.17
implies that
for
S\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu}
and
S^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\nu^{\prime}}
, the restriction of the
cup product
[\![S]\!]\smile[\![S^{\prime}]\!]
to the cohomology group
in the bottom right corner of Proposition
6.6
, can be identified with the
G
-invariant element

\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\cap S\times S^{\prime}}(a,b)\ \in\ \Big(\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}]\Big)^{G}\ \cong H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q}).

The following lemma concludes the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Lemma 6.18
.

There is the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}),\partial^{\mathrm{fat}});\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]_{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\![-]\!]\cdot[\![-]\!]}

Here the vertical maps are the canonical isomorphisms, given by the bases of the top homology
groups of elements of the form
[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{l}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{a},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]\otimes[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}^{\mathrm{r}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{b},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]
,
for
(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, and, respectively, of the form
[\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{c},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}]
, for
c\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
.
Moreover,
[\![-]\!]\cdot[\![-]\!]
denotes the map
(a,b)\mapsto[\![a]\!]\cdot[\![b]\!]
.
Passing to
G
-invariants, we obtain the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}]\Big)^{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})_{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\![-]\!]\cdot[\![-]\!]}

Proof.

We first argue that commutativity of the first diagram implies commutativity of the second.
The Serre spectral sequences computing the cohomology of the couples of bundles
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})

and
(\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathfrak{F}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}})
have both a second page concentrated in the
single entry in position
(0,2\nu+\nu^{\prime})
,
with value, respectively, the
G
-invariants

H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}},\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}};\mathbb{Q})^{G}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad H^{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}(\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}),\partial^{\mathrm{fat}});\mathbb{Q})^{G}.

This implies that the spectral sequences collapse and
that the top row of the second diagram is obtained from the top row of the first diagram by taking
G
-invariants.
This, together with the fact that all arrows in the first diagram are
G
-equivariant, shows that the second
diagram is obtained from the first by taking
G
-invariants.

It suffices therefore to prove commutativity of the first diagram.
Recall that
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
is an orientable
\mathbb{Q}
-homology manifold; moreover,
both differences
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

and
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
are relatively compact
inside
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
. We can therefore apply Poincaré-Lefschetz duality
and reduce to proving commutativity of the following diagram, where we use the abbreviation
\mathrm{Hur}
for

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{0}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{0}(\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}};\mathbb{Q})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}\left<\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}\right>[0]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bigoplus_{c\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}}\mathbb{Q}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\![-]\!]\cdot[\![-]\!]}

Here the bottom right group is abstractly isomorphic to
\mathbb{Q}[\mathcal{Q}]_{2\nu+2\nu^{\prime}}
, but lives naturally in degree 0.
The vertical isomorphisms are given as follows:

•

the left vertical map comes from the identification of
\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
with

\coprod_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,r}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{b}

induced by the maps
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,1/2}}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{1/2,1}}
; the second space
has connected components in bijection with
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, by taking the total monodromies of the two factors;

•

the right vertical map comes from the identification of
\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
with

\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}};

the second space has connected components in bijection with
\mathcal{Q}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
by taking the total monodromy.

Commutativity of the last diagram follows from the observation that for all
(a,b)\in\mathcal{Q}_{\nu,\nu^{\prime}}
, if we set

c=ab\in\mathcal{Q}
, then the inclusion of
\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial_{\mathrm{lr}}^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}

into
\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}\setminus\partial^{\mathrm{fat}}\mathrm{Hur}_{\nu+\nu^{\prime}}
restricts to an inclusion

\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,l}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{a}\times\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{7z_{\mathrm{c,r}}}{8}+\frac{1}{8}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{b}\subset\mathrm{Hur}\left(\frac{z_{\mathrm{c}}}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\mathcal{R}};\mathcal{Q}_{+}\right)_{c}.

∎

6.5.
Stable rational cohomology of classical Hurwitz spaces

We apply Theorem
6.1
in the case of a finite
PMQ
\mathcal{Q}
with trivial multiplication: recall from
[
Bia21
, Example 6.20]
that every PMQ with trivial product
is Poincaré, in particular it is
\mathbb{Q}
-Poincaré.
The algebra
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
is isomorphic in this case to
\mathbb{Q}[x_{S}\,|\,S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})]/(x_{S}^{2})
,
i.e. the quotient of the polynomial ring over
\mathbb{Q}
with one variable
x_{S}
in degree two
for each conjugacy class
S\subset\mathcal{Q}_{+}
, modulo the ideal generated by the squares
x_{S}^{2}
.
A minimal Sullivan model for
\mathcal{A}(\mathcal{Q})
is given by the commutative differential graded algebra
(\mathbb{A}(\mathcal{Q}),d)
, where

\mathbb{A}(\mathcal{Q})=\mathbb{Q}[x_{S}\,|\,S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})]\otimes\Lambda_{\mathbb{Q}}[y_{S}\,|\,S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})],

with
x_{S}
in degree two and
y_{S}
in degree three, and where the unique non-trivial differentials
are
d(y_{S})=x_{S}^{2}
for all
S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})
.
Looping twice the minimal Sullivan model (i.e. decreasing all degrees of the
x_{S}
and
y_{S}
by 2),
and restricting to one connected component, we obtain that the rational cohomology
of
\Omega_{0}B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q})
is isomorphic to
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Q}}[y^{\prime}_{S}\,|\,S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})]
,
i.e. it is the free exterior algebra over
\mathbb{Q}
generated by classes
y^{\prime}_{S}
in degree one,
one for each
S\in\mathrm{conj}(\mathcal{Q}_{+})
: the class
y^{\prime}_{S}
is obtained by looping twice
y_{S}\in\mathbb{A}(\mathcal{Q})
.

There is a special case of interest, namely when
\mathcal{Q}
has the form
c\sqcup\left\{\mathbbl{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}\right\}
,
for
c\subset G
a finite, conjugacy invariant subset of a group
G
: then by
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 7.3]
the space
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}(\mathcal{Q})
is homotopy equivalent to the topological monoid

\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G}:=\coprod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G,n}
of classical Hurwitz spaces with monodromies in
c
,
see
[
EVW16
, Subsection 2.6]
and
[
RW19
, Subsection 4.2]
. Adding a disjoint
unit, we obtain a homotopy equivalence of topological monoids

\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q})\simeq\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}\sqcup\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G}
; we can now recall that the weak homotopy type of the group completion of a topological monoid does not change up to homotopy if we add a disjoint unit to the monoid and, thus, we obtain
weak equivalences

\Omega B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}(\mathcal{Q})\simeq\Omega B\left(\left\{\mathbbl{1}\right\}\sqcup\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G}\right)\simeq\Omega B\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G}.

Thus we obtain a computation of the rational cohomology of
\Omega B\mathrm{Hur}^{c}_{G}
,
which is computed already in
[
RW19
, Corollary 5.4]
;
a claim of the result already appears in the withdrawn preprint
[
EVW12
]
,
as a combination of the statement of
[
EVW12
, Theorem 2.8.1]
for
n=2
and
X=BG
, and the discussion in
[
EVW12
, Subsection 5.6]
. See also the conjecture in

[
EVW16
, Subsection 1.5]
, which predicts the previous computation for
c
being the conjugacy class of transpositions in a symmetric group on at least three letters.

Appendix A
Deferred proofs

A.1.
Proof of Proposition
2.13

The two cases
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
and
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
are analogous, so we will
focus on the case of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
, which is slightly more difficult.

Recall Definition
2.4
. We define in a symmetric way
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{-}

as the subspace of
[0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial})

containing couples
(t,\mathfrak{c})
with
\mathfrak{c}
supported in
\mathring{\mathbb{S}}_{-t,0}
.

Note that
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{-}
is contained in the subspace

[0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}},\breve{\partial})
,
where we define
(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}},\breve{\partial})
as the nice couple

\big((-\infty,0)\times[0,1],(-\infty,0)\times\left\{0,1\right\}\big)
.

By
[
Bia23a
, Proposition 4.4]
the assignment
(t,\mathfrak{c})\mapsto(t,(\tau_{-t})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}))

gives a continous map
\tau^{-}\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to[0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial})
;
note that
\tau^{-}
has values in the subspace
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{-}
; in fact
\tau^{-}
is a homeomorphism

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\cong\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{-}
.

Recall
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.16]
. The following composition of continous
maps takes values in

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\subset[0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial})

and it coincides with
\mu\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\times\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
as a map of sets:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\times\breve{\mathrm{HM}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{-}\times\breve{\mathrm{HM}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}},\breve{\partial})\times\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,0}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}},\breve{\partial})\times\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,\infty}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,\infty}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}}\cup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}^{-}}\cup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[0,\infty\right)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{-}\times\Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Id\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,0}}\times\Id\times\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\sqcup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subset}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tau}}

Here, by abuse of notation, we denote by
-\sqcup-
the map
(t,\mathfrak{c},t^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})\mapsto(t,t^{\prime},\mathfrak{c}\sqcup\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
;
the map
\hat{\tau}
is defined by
(t,t^{\prime},\mathfrak{c})\mapsto(t+t^{\prime},(\tau_{t})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}))
. This shows continuity
of the product
\mu
.

To prove associativity of
\mu
, let
\mathfrak{c}_{i}=(P_{i},\psi_{i},\varphi_{i})
be a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}},\breve{\partial})

for
i=1,2,3
.
Under the identification
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{0,t_{i}}}

we can regard
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
as a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,t_{i}}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}},\breve{\partial})
.
Then
(\tau_{t_{1}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{2})

is a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{t_{1},t_{1}+t_{2}}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{2}}+t_{1},\breve{\partial})

and
(\tau_{t_{1}+t_{2}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{3})
is a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{t_{1}+t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{3}}+t_{1}+t_{2},\breve{\partial})
).
The compositions

\mathfrak{c}_{1}\sqcup\big((\tau_{t_{1}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{2})\sqcup(\tau_{t_{1}+t_{2}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{3})\big)

and
\big(\mathfrak{c}_{1}\sqcup(\tau_{t_{1}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{2})\big)\sqcup(\tau_{t_{1}+t_{2}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{3})

represent the same configuration in

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}}}\left(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}\cup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{2}}+t_{1}\cup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{3}}+t_{1}+t_{2},\breve{\partial}\right)
,
and by inclusion and change of ambient to
\mathbb{C}
, the same configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}},\breve{\partial})
.
This proves associativity of
\mu
.

The fact that
(0,(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1}))
is a two-sided unit for
\mu
follows directly from Definition
2.11
, together with the fact that
\tau_{0}
is the identity of
\mathbb{C}
.

A.2.
Proof of Lemma
2.18

We start by proving that the elements
\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})
generate
\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})
as a monoid.
Let
(t,\mathfrak{c})\in\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
and use Notation
2.2
.
If
P=\emptyset
, then we can continuously
reduce
t
to 0, so that
\pi_{0}(t,\mathfrak{c})=\pi_{0}(0,(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1}))
is
the neutral element of
\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})
.
Suppose from now on that
P\neq\emptyset
.

Suppose first that
P=\left\{z\right\}
consists of a single point.
By Lemma
2.7
we can connect
(t,\mathfrak{c})
with a configuration
of the form
(1,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
;
we can then connect
(1,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
with a configuration
of the form
\mathfrak{c}_{a}
: for this we can use any homotopy of
\mathbb{C}
through semialgebraic
homeomorphisms which is at all times
supported on
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
(i.e.
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathring{\mathcal{R}}
is fixed pointwise at all times),
and pushes the unique point
z^{\prime}\in P^{\prime}
to
z_{\mathrm{c}}
.
It follows that
(t,\mathfrak{c})
and
(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})
are connected in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
.

Suppose now that
\left|P\right|\geq 2
. We can perturb the positions of the points
z_{i}\in P
inside

\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t}
and assume that their real parts
\Re(z_{i})
are all different: again, we use a
semialgebraic isotopy
of
\mathbb{C}
supported on
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t}
, starting from the identity of
\mathbb{C}
and ending with a
semialgebraic homeomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
mapping
P

to a set of points with distinct real parts.

Without loss of generality we assume that
P
already has this property and we also assume

\Re(z_{1})<\dots<\Re(z_{k})
; choose positive real numbers
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots<t_{k}=t
such that

t_{i-1}<\Re(z_{i})<t_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
In particular we can regard
\mathfrak{c}
as a configuration in

\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}\cup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{2}-t_{1}}+t_{1}\cup\dots\cup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{k}-t_{k-1}}+t_{k-1}).

Recall
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.15]
, and for all
1\leq i\leq k
let
\mathfrak{c}_{i}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}-t_{i-1}})
be the image of
\mathfrak{c}
along the following composition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}\cup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{2}-t_{1}}\!+\!t_{1}\cup\dots\cup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{k}-t_{k-1}}\!+\!t_{k-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\!+\!t_{i-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}\!+\!t_{i-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}-t_{i-1}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{i}_{\mathbb{S}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}}}^{\mathbb{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathfrak{i}_{\mathbb{S}_{t_{i-1},t_{i}}}^{\mathbb{C}})^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tau_{-t_{i-1}})_{*}}

Then
(t,\mathfrak{c})
is equal to the product
(t_{1}-t_{0},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{k}-t_{k-1},\mathfrak{c}_{k})

in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
, and each
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
is supported on the single point
\tau_{-t_{i-1}}(z_{i})
.
It follows that the elements
\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})
generate
\pi_{0}(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}})
as a monoid.

Now let
a,b\in\mathcal{Q}
, and note that
(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})\cdot(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})
has the form
(2,\mathfrak{c})
,
for some
\mathfrak{c}=(P,\psi)\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{2})
with

P=\left\{z_{1}=z_{\mathrm{c}},z_{2}=z_{\mathrm{c}}+1\right\}
.
Let
f_{1},f_{2}
