be the admissible generating set for
\mathfrak{G}(P)
with
f_{1}
represented by a loop supported on
\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,1}

and
f_{2}
represented by a loop supported on
\mathbb{S}_{1,+\infty}
.

We can fix a semialgebraic isotopy

\mathcal{H}\colon\mathbb{C}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{C}
supported on
\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
,
starting from the identity of
\mathbb{C}
and swapping at time 1 the two points
of
P
according to a clockwise half Dehn twist. We use the notation
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(P,\psi^{\prime}):=\mathcal{H}_{*}(\mathfrak{c};1)\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{2})
.

The homeomorphism
\mathcal{H}(-,1)\colon\mathbb{C}\setminus P\to\mathbb{C}\setminus P
induces an automorphism of the fundamental group

\mathcal{H}(-,1)_{*}\colon\mathfrak{G}(P)\to\mathfrak{G}(P)
which restricts to an automorphism
of the fundamental PMQ
\mathcal{H}(-,1)_{*}\colon\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(P)\to\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(P)
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}(-,1)_{*}
sends
f_{2}\mapsto f_{1}
and
f_{1}^{f_{2}}\mapsto f_{2}
.
By definition we have
\psi^{\prime}=\psi\circ\mathcal{H}(-,1)_{*}^{-1}
, hence
\psi^{\prime}(f_{1})=\psi(f_{2})=b

and
\psi^{\prime}(f_{2})=\psi(f_{1}^{f_{2}})=a^{b}
. It follows that

(P,\psi^{\prime})=(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})\cdot(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a^{b}})
,
hence
\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})\cdot\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})=\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})\cdot\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a^{b}})
.

Suppose now that the product
ab
is defined in
\mathcal{Q}
. Again by Lemma
2.7
we can connect

(2,\mathfrak{c}):=(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})\cdot(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})
to a configuration of the form
(1,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime})
, with

\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}})
; for instance we can take
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}=\Lambda_{*}(\mathfrak{c},1/2)
,
where the semialgebraic isotopy
\Lambda\colon\mathbb{C}\times(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{C}
was introduced in the proof of Lemma
2.7
.
In fact, we have
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}})_{ab}
; by
[
Bia23a
, Corollary 6.5]
the space

\mathrm{Hur}_{+}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}})_{ab}
is contractible, in particular it is connected; hence
\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{a})\cdot\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{b})=\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime\prime})=\pi_{0}(1,\mathfrak{c}_{ab})
.

A.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.3

Definition A.1
.

We introduce a subspace
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}\subset\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
. A couple
(t,\mathfrak{c})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
belongs to
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}

if
t\geq 1
and the point
z^{\flat}_{t}:=t-\frac{1}{2}
belongs
to the support of
\mathfrak{c}
.

Note that
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
is invariant under the action of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
by left multiplication.

Definition A.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathbbl{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}},\breve{\partial})
be as in the proof of Lemma
3.2
,
and note that
(1,\mathfrak{c}_{\mathbbl{1}}^{\mathrm{d}})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
; in fact,
the right multiplication map
\mu(-;(1,\mathfrak{c}_{\mathbbl{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}))\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to\breve{\mathrm{HM}}

has image inside
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
. We define
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}\subset\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}

as the image of
\mu(-;(1,\mathfrak{c}_{\mathbbl{1}}^{\mathrm{d}}))
.

*
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
z^{\flat}_{t}
\bullet
a_{1}
g_{2}
a_{3}
g_{4}\neq\mathbbl{1}
a_{5}
*
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
\bullet
z^{\flat}_{t}
a_{1}
g_{2}
a_{3}
g_{4}=\mathbbl{1}

Figure 11.
Two configurations in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
; note that only the right one belongs to
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}
.

See Figure
11
. Note that also the subspace
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}
is invariant under the action of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}

by left multiplication.
Moreover,
\mu(-,(1,\mathfrak{c}_{\mathbbl{1}}))\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\to\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}
is a homeomorphism
of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
-left modules, and
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}\subset\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
contains
precisely all couples
(t,\mathfrak{c})
such that, using Notation
2.2
, the following hold:

•

\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\}\subseteq P\subset\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\}\cup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t-1}
;

•

\psi\colon\mathfrak{Q}(P)\to\mathcal{Q}
factors through
\mathfrak{Q}(P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\})

along the point-forgetting map
\mathfrak{i}^{P}_{P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\}}\colon\mathfrak{Q}(P)\to\mathfrak{Q}(P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\})
,
see
[
Bia23a
, Notation 2.17]
;

•

similarly,
\varphi\colon\mathfrak{G}(P)\to G
factors through
\mathfrak{G}(P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\})

along
\mathfrak{i}^{P}_{P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\}}\colon\mathfrak{G}(P)\to\mathfrak{G}(P\setminus\left\{z^{\flat}_{t}\right\})
.

Passing to bar constructions, we obtain inclusions of spaces

B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+})\hookrightarrow B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})\hookrightarrow B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}).

The first space
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+})
is homeomorphic
to
E\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
and, hence, is contractible, as
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
is a unital monoid.
We will prove that the inclusions
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})\hookrightarrow B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+})

and
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+})\hookrightarrow B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})
are weak homotopy equivalences:
this will conclude the proof of Proposition
3.3
.

Lemma A.3
.

The inclusion
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}\subset\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
is a homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

The argument is similar to that of the proof of Lemma
3.2
, but a bit more care is needed.

We define a continous map

\mathscr{E}^{\flat}\colon(0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Ran}_{+}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty})\times[0,1]\to\mathrm{Ran}_{+}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty})

by the formula

\mathscr{E}^{\flat}(t,\left\{z_{1},\dots,z_{k}\right\},s)=\left\{(1-s)z_{1}+st,\dots,(1-s)z_{k}+st\right\}.

We also let
\mathfrak{j}\colon(0,\infty)\to(0,\infty)
be given by the formula

\mathfrak{j}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}t-\frac{1}{2}&\mbox{for }t\geq 1\\
\frac{t}{2}&\mbox{for }t\leq 1.\end{array}\right.

We consider then the homotopy
\mathcal{H}^{\flat}\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}\times[0,1]\to(0,\infty)\times\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial})
given by the formula

\mathcal{H}^{\flat}(t,\mathfrak{c};s)=\Big(t\ ,\ \mathfrak{c}\times\mathscr{E}^{\flat}\left(\mathfrak{j}(t),\varepsilon(\mathfrak{c}),s\right)\Big).

Let
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,t\geq 1}
denote the subspace of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
containing all couples

(t,\mathfrak{c})
with
t\geq 1
. Then the homotopy
\mathcal{H}^{\flat}
has the following properties:

•

\mathcal{H}^{\flat}(-,s)
sends
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
inside
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
for all
0\leq s\leq 1
;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\flat}(-,0)
is the identity of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\flat}(-,s)
preserves the subspaces
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
and
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,t\geq 1}
at all times;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\flat}(-,1)
sends
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,t\geq 1}
inside
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
.

This shows that the inclusion
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}\hookrightarrow\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,t\geq 1}
is a homotopy equivalence.
Moreover there is a deformation retraction of

\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
onto
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+,t\geq 1}
given by the formula

((t,\mathfrak{c});s)\mapsto\mu\big(\ (\max\left\{0,s-t\right\},(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1}))\ ,\ (t,\mathfrak{c})\ \big).

It follows that the inclusion
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}\subset\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+}
is a homotopy equivalence.
∎

By Lemma
A.3
the inclusion of semi-simplicial spaces

B_{\bullet}(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})\subset B_{\bullet}(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+})

is levelwise a homotopy equivalence; after (thick) geometric realisation we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary A.4
.

The inclusion
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})\subset B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+})
is a weak homotopy equivalence.

For the second step of the proof of Proposition
3.3

we define a homotopy

\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}\colon\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}\times[0,1]\to\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}

by setting

\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}\colon((t,\mathfrak{c});s)\mapsto(t+s,\mathfrak{c}\times\left\{z^{\flat}_{t+s}\right\})

for
0\leq s\leq 1
and
(t,\mathfrak{c})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
; here

\mathfrak{c}\times\left\{z^{\flat}_{t+s}\right\}
is the evaluation at

(\mathfrak{c},\left\{z^{\flat}_{t+s}\right\})
of the external product

-\times-\colon\mathrm{Hur}\left(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial}\right)\times\mathrm{Ran}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty})\to\mathrm{Hur}\left(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty},\breve{\partial}\right).

Roughly speaking, the homotopy
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
has the following effects on configurations

(t,\mathfrak{c})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
:

•

it increases by 1 the first component
t
of a couple
(t,\mathfrak{c})\in\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
;

•

it splits
z^{\flat}_{t}
, which already belongs to the support of
\mathfrak{c}
, into two points; one point remains at the position

z^{\flat}_{t}
and “keeps the local monodromy” (which is only defined as an element of
G
,
because
z^{\flat}_{t}\in\breve{\partial}\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\infty}
); the other point moves gradually
to a distance 1 to right and has trivial local monodromy
\mathbbl{1}

(also only defined as element of
G
).

Note that
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
has the following properties:

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}(-;0)
is the identity of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}
;

•

\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}(-;1)
has values inside
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+}
;

•

for all
0\leq s\leq 1
the map
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}(-;s)
is
equivariant with respect to the action of
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
on
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+}

by left multiplication.

As a consequence,
\mathcal{H}^{\sharp}
induces a homotopy of
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})
starting from the identity and ending with
a map
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})\to B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+})
.
We obtain the following lemma, which is the last step needed to prove Proposition
3.3
.

Lemma A.5
.

The space
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})
admits a deformation into
its contractible subspace
B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\sharp}_{+})
. As a consequence

B(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}_{+},\breve{\mathrm{HM}}^{\flat}_{+})
is weakly contractible.

A.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.15

We first give a rough idea of the proof: the value of
\check{\sigma}
at a given sequence
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
in
\Delta^{p}\times\left(\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}\right)^{p}
(respectively, in
\Delta^{p}\times\left(\breve{\mathrm{HM}}\right)^{p}
) is obtained by combining several steps: the first step is computing the product
\hat{\mu}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
in
\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(in
\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
). The product map
\hat{\mu}
, however, does not factor through
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively,
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
): one of the reasons is that if
w_{0}=0
, the sequence
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
is equivalent in
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(respectively, in
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
) to the sequence obtained by forgetting
w_{0}
and
(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})
; yet the product

(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
is, in general, different from the product
(t_{2},\mathfrak{c}_{2})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
. In fact, letting
0\leq i_{\min}\leq i_{\max}\leq p
be as in the discussion before Definition
4.5
, we might reduce
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
to an equivalent sequence
(w_{i_{\min}},\dots,w_{i_{\max}};t_{i_{\min}+1},\dots,t_{i_{\max}};\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\min}+1},\dots,\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\max}})
, yet the product
(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
is, in general, different from the subproduct
(t_{i_{\min}+1},\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\min}+1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{i_{\max}},\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\max}})
.

We solve this problem by using the barycentres
b^{-}_{\varepsilon}<b^{+}_{\varepsilon}
, and by considering only the part of the configuration
(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
lying in the strip
\mathbb{S}_{b^{-}_{\varepsilon},b_{\varepsilon}^{+}}
: more precisely, using a suitable expansion and translation (implemented via the homotopies
\tau_{*}
and
\Lambda_{*}
), we map the strip
\mathbb{S}_{b^{-}_{\varepsilon},b_{\varepsilon}^{+}}
to the strip
\mathbb{S}_{0,1}
, and then we collapse the two parts of the obtained configuration lying outside the strip
\mathbb{S}_{0,1}
: we first collapse all points lying on left (respectively, on right) of
\mathbb{S}_{0,1}
to the segment
0\times[0,1]
(respectively,
1\times[0,1]
) via the homotopies
\kappa^{+}_{*}
and
\kappa^{-}_{*}
, we further collapse these vertical segments to the two points
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
via
(\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1})_{*}
, and finally we get rid of the residual information of the
G
-valued local monodromy at
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
by quotienting by the action of
G\times G^{op}
. The key observation is that the part of
(t_{1},\mathfrak{c}_{1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{p},\mathfrak{c}_{p})
lying in the strip
\mathbb{S}_{b^{-}_{\varepsilon},b_{\varepsilon}^{+}}
only depends on the subproduct
(t_{i_{\min}+1},\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\min}+1})\cdot\dots\cdot(t_{i_{\max}},\mathfrak{c}_{i_{\max}})
. The conditions on
\varepsilon
, moreover, ensure that either
\varepsilon=0
, i.e.
b_{\varepsilon}^{-}=b^{-}
and
b_{\varepsilon}^{+}=b^{+}
, or the part of
\hat{\mu}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
lying inside
\mathbb{S}_{b^{-}_{\varepsilon},b_{\varepsilon}^{+}}
is “empty” and, in particular, independent of the positive value of
\varepsilon
; this is the rough reason why
\check{\sigma}
and, hence,
\sigma
and
\bar{\sigma}
, do not depend on
\varepsilon
.

Before starting the proof of Proposition
4.15
, we give a definition.

Definition A.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{C}
be a nice couple, let
\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hur}(\mathfrak{C};\mathcal{Q},G)
be two configurations, and let
*\in\mathbb{T}\subset\mathbb{C}
be a contractible subspace. We say that
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}

agree on
\mathbb{T}
if the following hold, using Notation
2.2
:

•

P\cap\mathbb{T}=P^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{T}
;

•

for every loop
\gamma\subset\mathbb{T}\setminus P
representing an element

[\gamma]
in
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathfrak{C}}(P)
we have
\psi(\gamma)=\psi^{\prime}(\gamma)
;

•

for every loop
\gamma\subset\mathbb{T}\setminus P
representing an element

[\gamma]
in
\mathfrak{G}(P)
we have
\varphi(\gamma)=\varphi^{\prime}(\gamma)
).

Consider the particular case in which
\mathfrak{C}
splits as a disjoint union of nice couples
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2}

with
\mathfrak{C}_{1}\subset\mathring{\mathbb{T}}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
contained in the interior of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{T}
:
then
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.15]
gives configurations
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{c})
and

\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{C}_{1};\mathcal{Q},G)
, and the condition that

\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
agree on
\mathbb{T}
is equivalent to the equality
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{c})=\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
.

Now let
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
be as in Notation
4.2
and assume

w_{j}=0
for a fixed
0\leq j\leq p
;
let
(\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=d_{j}(\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
(see Definition
3.1
), let
\underline{w}^{\prime}
be obtained from
\underline{w}
by removing the (vanishing)
j
th
coordinate,
and present
\underline{w}^{\prime}
as
(w^{\prime}_{0},\dots,w^{\prime}_{p-1})
,
\underline{t}^{\prime}
as
(t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{p-1})
and
\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
as
(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{p-1})
.
We want to prove that
\check{\sigma}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=\check{\sigma}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
: this
implies that
\check{\sigma}
descends to a map
\sigma
defined on
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(on
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).

Let
a_{0},\dots,a_{p},b,b^{+},b^{-}
be computed as in Definition
4.4
with respect
to
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
and, similarly, let
a^{\prime}_{0},\dots,a^{\prime}_{p-1},b^{\prime},(b^{+})^{\prime},(b^{-})^{\prime}
be computed with respect
to
(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. We observe that
\varepsilon(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=0
if and only if the support
P
of
\hat{\mu}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
intersects non-trivially the strip
\mathbb{S}_{b^{-},b^{+}}
. Similarly,
\varepsilon(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=0
if and only if the support
P^{\prime}
of
\hat{\mu}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
intersects non-trivially
\mathbb{S}_{(b^{-})^{\prime},(b^{+})^{\prime}}
. We now observe that for
j>0
we have
P\cap\mathbb{S}_{b^{-},b^{+}}=P^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{S}_{(b^{-})^{\prime},(b^{+})^{\prime}}
, whereas for
j=0
we have
\tau(P\cap\mathbb{S}_{b^{-},b^{+}},-t_{1})=P^{\prime}\cap\mathbb{S}_{(b^{-})^{\prime},(b^{+})^{\prime}}
. In particular either intersection is empty if and only if the other is, or in other words,
\varepsilon(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})>0
if and only if
\varepsilon(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})>0
. In this case, however, we have that both
\check{\sigma}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
and
\check{\sigma}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
coincide with the basepoint
\mathfrak{c}^{\mathrm{lr}}
of
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{op}}
(respectively, of
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{op}}
) and, in particular, they are equal. This proves also the first statement of Proposition
4.15
.

From now on we assume
\varepsilon(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=\varepsilon(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=0
, allowing us to use the lower and upper barycentres instead of their
\varepsilon
-variations in the computations of the rest of the argument.
Note that
b-b^{-}=b^{\prime}-(b^{-})^{\prime}
and
b^{+}-b=(b^{+})^{\prime}-b^{\prime}
.
In particular, in the following we assume
b^{+}-b^{-}=(b^{+})^{\prime}-(b^{-})^{\prime}>0
.

First, we give an alternative description of the configuration
\hat{\mu}^{b}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
.
We regard
\mathfrak{c}_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq p
, as a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,t_{i}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}})

(in
\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{0,t_{i}}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}},\breve{\partial})
), and consider the configuration

(\tau_{a_{i-1}-b^{-}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{i})\in\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{i-1}-b^{-},a_{i}-b^{-}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}}+a_{i}-b^{-})

\left(\mbox{respectively, }(\tau_{a_{i-1}-b^{-}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{i})\in\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{i-1}-b^{-},a_{i}-b^{-}}}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{i}}+a_{i}-b^{-},\breve{\partial})\right).

Using the disjoint union map
-\sqcup-
from
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.16]
we obtain a configuration

\mathfrak{c}:=(\tau_{a_{0}-b^{-}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{1})\sqcup\dots\sqcup(\tau_{a_{p-1}-b^{-}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}_{p}),

which
a priori
belongs to

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{0}-b^{-},a_{p}-b^{-}}}\big(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{p}}+a_{p-1}-b^{-}\big)

\Big(\mbox{respectively, }\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{0}-b^{-},a_{p}-b^{-}}}\big(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{p}}+a_{p-1}-b^{-},\breve{\partial}\big)\Big),

but can be naturally considered as a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}})
(respectively,
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathbb{R}},\breve{\partial})
).
The equality
(\tau_{-b^{-}})_{*}(\hat{\mu}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}))=\mathfrak{c}

follows directly from Definitions
2.11
and
4.6
. It follows then from Definition

4.6
that
\hat{\mu}^{b}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
is equal to

\Lambda_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c};\frac{1}{b^{+}-b^{-}}\right)
. In a similar way, we obtain a configuration

\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}:=(\tau_{a^{\prime}_{0}-(b^{-})^{\prime}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{1})\sqcup\dots\sqcup(\tau_{a^{\prime}_{p-2}-(b^{-})^{\prime}})_{*}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{p-1}),

which we consider as a configuration in
\mathrm{Hur}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}{R})
(in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\mathcal{R}}{R},\breve{\partial})
), and identifications

\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=(\tau_{-(b^{-})^{\prime}})_{*}(\hat{\mu}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}))
and
\hat{\mu}^{b}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=\Lambda_{*}\left(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime};\frac{1}{(b^{+})^{\prime}-(b^{-})^{\prime}}\right)

Lemma A.7
.

The configurations
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
agree on the vertical strip
[0,b^{+}-b^{-}]\times\mathbb{R}
.

Proof.

We use Notation
2.2
and argue the statement differently, depending on the value of
j
.

•

If
1\leq j\leq p-1
, then
\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
.

•

If
j=0
, note that
a_{1}-b^{-}=a^{\prime}_{0}-(b^{-})^{\prime}\geq 0
and
a_{p}-b^{-}=a^{\prime}_{p-1}-(b^{-})^{\prime}\geq b^{+}-b^{-}
; then both
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
can be regarded as configurations in

\mathrm{Hur}\big(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p}-a_{1}}+a_{1}-b^{-}\big),

\Big(\mbox{respectively, }\mathrm{Hur}\big(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p}-a_{1}}+a_{1}-b^{-},\breve{\partial}\big)\Big),

and the restriction map
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{a_{1}-b^{-},a_{p}-b^{-}}}
from
[
Bia23a
, Definition 3.15]
sends
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
to the same configuration in the space

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{1}-b^{-},a_{p}-b^{-}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p}-a_{1}}+a_{1}-b^{-})\quad\Big(\mbox{respectively, }\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{a_{1}-b^{-},\infty}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p}-a_{1}}+a_{1}-b^{-})\Big);

this common image is essentially the configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
.
Now we use that
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}\subset\mathbb{S}_{a_{1}-b^{-},a_{p}-b^{-}}
to conclude that

\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
also agree on
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}
and, finally, we notice that agreeing on
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}
is equivalent to agreeing on
[0,b^{+}-b^{-}]\times\mathbb{R}
, as all configurations are supported in
\mathbb{H}
.

•

If
j=p
, note that
a_{0}=a^{\prime}_{0}
,
b^{-}=(b^{-})^{\prime}
and
b^{+}=(b^{+})^{\prime}\leq a_{p-1}=a^{\prime}_{p-1}
;
then both
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
can be regarded as configurations in

\mathrm{Hur}\big(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p-1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{p}}+a_{p-1}-b^{-}\big),

\Big(\mbox{respectively, }\mathrm{Hur}\big(\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p-1}}+a_{0}-b^{-}\sqcup\breve{\mathcal{R}}_{t_{p}}+a_{p-1}-b^{-},\breve{\partial}\big)\Big),

and
\mathfrak{i}^{\mathbb{C}}_{\mathbb{S}_{a_{0}-b^{-},a_{p-1}-b^{-}}}
sends
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}

to the same configuration in the space

\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,a_{p-1}-b^{-}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p-1}}+a_{0}-b^{-})

\Big(\mbox{respectively, }\mathrm{Hur}^{\mathbb{S}_{-\infty,a_{p-1}-b^{-}}}(\mathring{\mathcal{R}}_{a_{p-1}}+a_{0}-b^{-})\Big);

this common image is essentially the configuration
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
.
Now we use that
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}\subset\mathbb{S}_{a_{0}-b^{-},a_{p-1}-b^{-}}
to conclude that

\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}
also agree on
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}
, and again we note that agreeing on
\mathbb{S}_{0,b^{+}-b^{-}}
is equivalent to agreeing on
[0,b^{+}-b^{-}]\times\mathbb{R}
.

∎

By applying
\Lambda_{*}\left(-;\frac{1}{b^{+}-b^{-}}\right)=\Lambda_{*}\left(-;\frac{1}{(b^{+})^{\prime}-(b^{-})^{\prime}}\right)

we obtain that, in a similar way,
\hat{\mu}^{b}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
and
\hat{\mu}^{b}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})

agree on the vertical strip
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
.

Note that the vertical strip
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
is preserved by the homotopies
\kappa^{-},\kappa^{+}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}
at all times,
and the homotopies
\kappa^{-},\kappa^{+}
restrict even to the identity on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
.
This, together with Lemma
4.12
, implies that

\hat{\mu}^{\diamond}_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}\mathfrak{c}:=\tilde{\sigma}(w_{i};t_{i},\mathfrak{c}_{i})
and

\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}:=\hat{\mu}^{\diamond}_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}(w^{\prime}_{i};t^{\prime}_{i},\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{i})

are both supported on the set
\tilde{P}:=\mathcal{H}^{\diamond}_{1}(P)\cup\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}
. Let
\tilde{k}=|\tilde{P}|
and
\tilde{l}=|P\setminus\partial\diamond|
.

Recall that
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}=(\tilde{P},\tilde{\psi},\tilde{\varphi})
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}=(\tilde{P},\tilde{\psi}^{\prime},\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime})

are configurations in
\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
(in
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{\breve{\partial}\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}}}
).
We can choose an lr-based admissible generating set
\tilde{f}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}_{\tilde{k}}
of
\mathfrak{G}(\tilde{P})
(see
[
Bia23a
, Definition 6.10]
) with the following properties:

•

two generators, denoted
\tilde{f}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
\tilde{f}^{\mathrm{r}}
, are represented by loops spinning clockwise around
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
z_{\diamond}^{\mathrm{r}}
respectively;

•

the other generators are represented by loops contained in the strip
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
;
in particular, we assume that
\tilde{f}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}_{\tilde{l}}
correspond
to points in
P\setminus\partial\diamond
.

Since
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
agree on
[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
, we have that
\tilde{\psi}
and
\tilde{\psi}^{\prime}
agree on the admissible generators

\tilde{f}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}_{\tilde{l}}
, whereas

\tilde{\varphi}
and
\tilde{\varphi}^{\prime}
agree on all admissible generators

\tilde{f}_{1},\dots,\tilde{f}_{\tilde{k}}
except, possibly, the two generators
\tilde{f}^{\mathrm{l}}
and
\tilde{f}^{\mathrm{r}}
.
It follows that
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
have the same image in the quotient

\mathrm{Hur}(\breve{\diamond}^{\mathrm{lr}},\breve{\partial})_{G,G^{op}}
(respectively,
\mathrm{Hur}(\diamond,\partial)_{G,G^{op}}
),
i.e.
\check{\sigma}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=\check{\sigma}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. This concludes the proof that
\check{\sigma}
descends to a map
\sigma
defined on
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(on
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).

For the second statement of the proposition, let
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
be as in Notation
4.2
and assume
(t_{j},\mathfrak{c}_{j})=(0,(\emptyset,\mathbbl{1},\mathbbl{1}))
for a fixed
0\leq j\leq p
;
let
(\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})=d_{j}(\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
, so that vice versa
(\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=s_{j}(\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
, and let
\underline{w}^{\prime}
be obtained from
\underline{w}
by replacing the consecutive entries
w_{j}
and
w_{j+1}
with their sum
w_{j}+w_{j+1}
;
present
\underline{w}^{\prime}
as
(w^{\prime}_{0},\dots,w^{\prime}_{p-1})
,
\underline{t}^{\prime}
as
(t^{\prime}_{1},\dots,t^{\prime}_{p-1})
and
\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
as
(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{p-1})
.
We want to prove that
\check{\sigma}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=\check{\sigma}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
: this
implies that
\check{\sigma}
descends to a map
\sigma
defined on
B\mathring{\mathrm{HM}}
(on
B\breve{\mathrm{HM}}
).

For this it suffices to note that
\hat{\mu}(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})=\hat{\mu}(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
and also the barycentres

b,b^{-},b^{+}
are equal when computed with respect to
(\underline{w};\underline{t},\underline{\mathfrak{c}})
or
(\underline{w}^{\prime};\underline{t}^{\prime},\underline{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime})
. The formula for
\check{\sigma}
only depends on these parameters.
